# Architecture review, 2026-11-11

Range: 2026-11-02 to 2026-11-11. Commits reviewed: Wbskt 170, Console 25, Python 20, .NET 36, Arduino 13, site 25.
Production numbers: not available (code only). The export asked for in #619 is not in yet, so every index and lock finding comes from the code alone.

Follow-ups epic: #730. Previous review: 2026-11-02 (epic #537, report in the project files).

## Summary

Nine days and 289 commits added billing through Dodo, plans and credit metering, tenants and invitations, an MQTT gateway, the move to `/api/v1`, server-side drafts and test runs, and the first releases of the three client libraries. Ten lanes raised 155 findings; after merging the ones two lanes both found there are 151: 6 P1, 59 P2 and 86 P3. The P1s are narrow and cheap to fix: the master pipeline is red on three jobs and the last deploy failed, workspace creation picks the tenant for the caller, an MQTT reconnect can leave a client connected with no session (medium confidence, not run), the Python client hangs or exits in its own README samples, and the console's Runs tab filters on a status the engine never writes. The P2s gather where the new money paths meet a failure: credits and caps that can be passed or lose their notices, billing states nothing repairs when a webhook or a refund call is lost, presence that stays wrong after a broker outage, and alerting that all runs on the VM it watches. Of the 2 Nov findings, H1, H2, H4, M1 to M5, M8, M9 and L7 are fixed, H3 is fixed for the message handler only (finding 35), M6 is open as #547, M7 is mostly fixed, and L8 is carried into #784.

### How this was ranked and filed

- P1 breaks users or loses data now, P2 should be fixed before going public, P3 is cleanup.
- A P1 or P2 finding has its own sub-issue of #730 (P1 in M2, P2 in M3 Go public). P3 findings are grouped into one "small items" issue per lane (Post-launch). Console and site findings are filed in wbskt/Wbskt.Console and attached to #730; library findings are tracked in wbskt/Wbskt.
- One finding was moved up in the merge: the red master pipeline was raised from P2 to P1, because nothing merges green or deploys until it is fixed.
- Four findings were raised by two lanes and are listed once: the home page's "month of readings" (console and site, billing), the console's missing policy and client listeners (console and site, auth and management), browser-only drafts (console and site, workflow engine) and top-level declarations in console services (console and site, tenants).
- `Workspace_Retire` deleting the `dbo.WorkspaceAccounts` row is the one root cause behind findings 10 and 48; they are kept apart because the fixes differ.

### 1. Workspace creation picks the tenant for the caller, with no choice and no permission check

P1 · Tenants and access · size M · #731

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/WorkspaceService.cs:54-56 (takes `tenants.FirstOrDefault()`, under a TODO that says "today a user has exactly one tenant"); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/Tenant_GetForUser.sql:7-11 (no `ORDER BY`); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Controllers/WorkspacesController.cs:53-64 (no `[RequiresTenantPermission]`); Models/Wbskt.Auth.Models/WorkspaceDtos.cs (`CreateWorkspaceRequest` has no tenant reference); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Create.sql:38-44 (creator gets the tenant's Admin role scoped to the workspace)

**Scenario:** Every invited user is in two tenants, because registration always creates a personal one (Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/RegistrationService.cs:119-123) and the invitation adds the second. Request sequence: (1) owner A of a Maker tenant T_A sends `POST /api/v1/tenants/{T_A}/invitations` for B with the read-only `User` role; (2) B registers with the token, or accepts it; (3) B sends `POST /api/v1/workspaces {"name":"x"}`. The server takes the first row `Tenant_GetForUser` returns. With no `ORDER BY` and no index on `TenantMembers.UserId` that is most likely the lower tenant id, which is T_A for anyone who registered after T_A existed. The workspace is created in T_A, counts against T_A's workspace limit, client pool and credits, and B is its owner with Admin there, although B holds only the read-only role in T_A and A never agreed. The reverse also breaks: B, who pays for Maker on the personal tenant, can never add a workspace to it through the API, and gets `PLAN_WORKSPACE_LIMIT_REACHED` for T_A instead. The E2E scenario `WS_CRD_04` (Tests/Wbskt.E2E.FeatureTests/Scenarios/Auth/WorkspaceLifecycleTests.cs:61-83) assumes "creating a workspace there" but never asserts which tenant the workspace landed in, so it passes either way.

**Fix:** Add `tenantRef` to `CreateWorkspaceRequest` (or move the route to `POST /api/v1/tenants/{tenantRef}/workspaces`), resolve it through `Tenant_FindBy_RefIdForUser`, and require a tenant-wide permission to create a workspace in a tenant (a new `workspaces.create`, or `users.manage`). Until the console sends it, default to the tenant where the caller is an administrator and refuse when that is ambiguous. Add `ORDER BY t.Id` to `Tenant_GetForUser` so the list is stable.

**Confidence:** high that the tenant is not chosen by the caller and that no permission is checked (second read of the service, controller, request record and procedure). Medium on which tenant wins, since the order is whatever plan SQL Server picks; it could not be run here.

### 2. An MQTT client that reconnects without clean session over a connection the gateway still holds ends up connected but with no session: everything it publishes is dropped

P1 · Gateways · size S · #732

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Services/MqttGateway.cs:49-51` (`WithPersistentSessions(false)` only), `:98` (the new `MqttConnection` is put in `args.SessionItems`), `:104-114` (`StartSessionAsync` reads the connection back from the session's items), `:146-164`; `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Infrastructure/MqttConnection.cs:84-94, 117-125`; `Docs/Client.Protocol.MQTT.md:28`

**Scenario:** A client connects with clean session (3.1.1) or clean start (5) off. Paho Python does this by default on every reconnect of an MQTT 5 client (`clean_start=MQTT_CLEAN_START_FIRST_ONLY`). Its Wi-Fi drops, it reconnects within a few seconds, and the gateway still holds the old TCP connection (the usual case: the server only notices after 1.5 keep-alives). MQTTnet then takes the connection over and, because the CONNECT did not ask for a clean session, **reuses the existing `MqttSession`** (`MqttClientSessionsManager.CreateClientConnection`: `session = oldSession`). `MqttSession.Items` is fixed at creation, so the session still carries the old `MqttConnection`; the one `ValidateConnectionAsync` just built is discarded. MQTTnet raises the takeover disconnect (the old `MqttConnection` is marked `Ended(Superseded)` and its channel completed), sends CONNACK success, then raises `ClientConnectedAsync` with the reused items. `StartSessionAsync` therefore starts a session over the already-ended connection: `ReceiveAsync` returns `Superseded` at once, the session unregisters, and no disconnect is announced (superseded is not "offline"). The client now holds a healthy MQTT connection whose PINGREQs are answered, but `ReceivePublishAsync` hands every PUBLISH to the ended connection, where `EnqueueAsync` swallows `ChannelClosedException`. QoS 1 publishes still get PUBACK success. Commands fail with "could not be sent" or wait in the queue. Nothing recovers it until the TCP connection breaks. `WithPersistentSessions(false)` does not prevent this: it only deletes a session after an ordinary disconnect, not during a takeover. The doc promises the opposite ("The gateway keeps no session between connections, whatever is asked").

**Fix:** Make the connection the gateway built for this CONNECT the one the session runs on, whatever MQTTnet did with the session. Smallest: in `StartSessionAsync`, when the connection found in the session items has already ended (`!connection.IsOpen`), build a fresh `MqttConnection` from its admission, store it in `args.SessionItems[SessionItemKey]` and run that. Add a loopback test to `MqttGatewayTests` that takes a connection over with `WithCleanSession(false)` and then publishes.

**Confidence:** medium. Second read confirmed the gateway side line by line and the MQTTnet side in `MqttClientSessionsManager.cs` and `MqttSession.cs` on MQTTnet `master`. Not run; the pinned 5.2.0.1603 could differ from `master`. Every existing test connects with `WithCleanSession()` (`Tests/Wbskt.Host.Tests/Gateway/Mqtt/MqttGatewayTests.cs:281`), so nothing proves otherwise.

### 3. Python sync client hangs for good when a handler calls send, which the README's own Commands sample does

P1 · Client libraries · size S · #733

**Where:** src/wbskt/client.py:440 (handlers run on the loop thread); src/wbskt/client.py:427-428 and 450-451 (`send` blocks on `run_coroutine_threadsafe(...).result()`); README.md:62-74 (the sample)

**Scenario:** A script uses `WbsktClient` and, as README.md:68-71 shows, calls `client.send("relay.state", ...)` inside `on_message`. The handler runs on the client's own event-loop thread, and `send` then waits on that same loop for a coroutine the loop can no longer run. Reproduced against the conformance fake: after one `relay.set` command the server received `capabilities` and `sys.ack` and nothing else. The `relay.state` message was never sent, a following `sys.ping` got no pong, a second message was never handed over, and `client.send` from the main thread blocked as well. The gateway then drops the client for missing pings and the frozen loop never reconnects, so the device stays offline until the process is restarted. `report_state` and `set_capabilities` from a handler hang the same way. This is in 0.9.0b1 on PyPI.

**Fix:** In `WbsktClient._call`, when the caller is already on the client's thread, schedule the coroutine on the loop without waiting for it (a send only queues). Or run sync handlers on a worker thread. Add a test to tests/test_sync_client.py that sends from a handler; the one test there (line 46) does not.

**Confidence:** high (run: handler trace `[('enter', 'relay.set', 'wbskt')]` with no exit, frames `['capabilities', 'sys.ack']`, main-thread send blocked for 3 s).

### 4. Python quick start exits before it connects: nothing is sent, and every run registers one more client

P1 · Client libraries · size XS · #734

**Where:** Wbskt.Python/README.md:18-26; site: app/src/app/pages/docs/sdks/sdks.html:42-52; Wbskt.Python/src/wbskt/client.py:401-407 (`start` returns at once; daemon thread on line 404)

**Scenario:** A user saves the quick start as a script and runs it. `start()` only schedules the first attempt on a daemon thread, `send` puts the reading in the offline buffer, and the script ends. Run three times against the conformance fake with `FileStorage`: each run exited in about 0.13 s, the API saw one registration per run (three in three runs), no login, no socket upgrade, no frame, and the credentials file was never written. So the reading is never sent, and each run leaves another registered client in the console that counts against the policy and the plan while its credentials are lost. Rated P1 because it is the first thing a Python user runs and it fails silently. The .NET quick start does not have this problem: `StartAsync` awaits the first attempt and `SendAsync` awaits the flush.

**Fix:** Change both samples to keep the process alive (a `while True: client.send(...); time.sleep(10)` loop, as the asyncio sample at README.md:49-51 already does). Better, make the sync `start()` wait for the first attempt as .NET does, and make `stop()` drain what it can.

**Confidence:** high (run, with both `MemoryStorage` and `FileStorage`). Over real TLS the registration call still completes, because the executor thread that makes it is joined at interpreter exit.

### 5. Runs tab uses the status "Completed", but the engine's word for a finished run is "Succeeded"

P1 · Console and site · size XS · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#169

**Where:** `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/models/run.model.ts:9` (filter chip sends `status=Completed`); `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/utils/run-display.util.ts:12,15` (`runTone` maps `'Completed'` to success, everything unknown to neutral); `workflow-tab/components/runs/workflow-runs.ts:54`; `workflow-tab/components/runs/run-details/run-details.ts:86,90`; backend `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Runtime/RunStatus.cs:18` (`Succeeded`), `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Tables/Runs.sql:36-39` (the CHECK constraint lists the only words a run can hold, and `Completed` is not one), `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_ListBy_WorkflowRefId.sql` (the filter is compared to the column as given)

**Scenario:** A workflow has runs that ended well. On its Runs tab the user presses the "Completed" chip: the console asks `GET .../runs?status=Completed`, no row can ever hold that word, and the list says "No Completed runs for this workflow". In the unfiltered list every successful run has status text "Succeeded" and gets the neutral grey tone, not the success colour, because `runTone('Succeeded')` falls to `default`. `Faulted`, which the same switch and `workflow-runs.ts:54` and `run-details.ts:90` test for, is a status no run ends in any more (`WorkflowStatsResponse.cs:4`).

**Fix:** Add a `RunStatus` enum in `run.model.ts` with the engine's eight words (Running, Failing, Cancelling, Succeeded, Failed, PartiallyFailed, Cancelled, OutOfCredits), use it in `RUN_STATUS_FILTERS` (label "Succeeded", value `Succeeded`) and in `runTone`; keep `Completed` only for branch statuses (`BranchStatus.Completed`), which `runTone` is also called with in `run-details.ts`.

**Confidence:** high (second read confirmed the CHECK constraint, the procedure's plain comparison and all console call sites; not run against a live API)

### 6. The master pipeline is red on three jobs, the last deploy failed, and nothing reported either

P1 · Infra and CI · size S · #735

**Where:** .github/workflows/build-images.yml:89 and :199 (`danielpalme/ReportGenerator-GitHub-Action@5`), :539-549 (the scan step, which prints project paths only at :545-546); Directory.Packages.props:26-28 (`Microsoft.AspNetCore.SignalR.StackExchangeRedis` 10.0.9 and the `MessagePack` 2.5.307 pin); .github/workflows/deploy.yml:96-99 (ssh with no `ConnectTimeout`, no retry); run 697 (master 556fa39, 11 Nov 14:00 UTC) and run 694 (master 5c7792c, 10 Nov 13:31 UTC) of "Build images"

**Scenario:** Run 697 on master failed three ways. `test` and `integration` failed in "Set up job" within two seconds: "Unable to resolve action `danielpalme/reportgenerator-github-action@5`, the provided ref `5` is the shortened version of a commit SHA, which is not supported". The tag `5` resolved on 10 Nov and does not on 11 Nov, so the upstream tag is gone. `scan` failed with "vulnerable NuGet packages found in 3 project(s)": `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host` and the two test projects that reference it, so the package is one only the management host brings in (the SignalR Redis backplane or the MessagePack it pulls; inferred, the log does not name it). The two pull-request runs of that day (695, 696) are green only because they changed docs and skipped every job; the next pull request that touches code fails three checks. One day earlier, run 694 passed every gate, pushed all six images and the migrator with `:latest`, and then `deploy / deploy` failed after 2 min 15 s with "ssh: connect to host *** port 22: Connection timed out"; it was not re-run. The last deploy that worked is run 692 (ec1e0d4, 9 Nov). The commits since are documentation, so production is not missing behaviour yet, but `:latest` (sha-5c7792c) is not what production runs, the VM did not answer on port 22 from GitHub and the cause is unknown, and no issue or alert records any of it.

**Fix:** Pin the coverage action to the full SHA the error message names (`5f3701a41d14548c9b8e575a5c235230e515cbc0`) at lines 89 and 199, and pin the rest while there (#460). Make the scan step print the package id, version and advisory from `vuln.json`, then bump or pin the flagged package. Re-run the deploy for the current master tag once the VM answers on port 22, and find out why it did not. In `deploy.yml` add `-o ConnectTimeout=20`, three attempts for the connection only, and a final step that raises an alert (or opens an issue) when the job fails.

**Confidence:** high for what failed (read from the job logs and the run list); medium for which package is flagged and for why the tag stopped resolving (the upstream repository could not be read from here)

### 7. Interface-declared `[SignalRPrivate]` ids are not stripped from the console feed

P2 · Auth and management · size XS · #736

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Program.cs:367-394 (the lookup at 384-386); Events/Wbskt.Events/Abstractions/IWorkspaceContext.cs:7, IClientContext.cs:5, IPolicyContext.cs, IWorkflowContext.cs, IUserContext.cs:5

**Scenario:** A console joined to a workspace receives `OnClientConnectedEvent`. `ClientConnectedEvent(Guid ClientRefId, int ClientId, int WorkspaceId, string HostId)` carries no attribute of its own: `[SignalRPrivate]` sits only on the interface properties. The modifier finds the interface property with `i.GetProperty(propertyInfo.Name)`, but `JsonPropertyInfo.Name` is the JSON name, and SignalR's payload options use camelCase, so it asks `IWorkspaceContext` for a property called `workspaceId`. `Type.GetProperty` is case-sensitive and returns null, so `clientId`, `workspaceId`, `policyId`, `workflowId` and `userId` go to the browser on every event whose record does not repeat the attribute (all the client, policy and workflow events). Only the properties that carry the attribute on the class itself (`ActorUserId`, `ClientAddress`, `UserAgent`, `WorkspaceIds`) are withheld. The conventions say a response never exposes an internal `Id`, and the console's GEMINI.md and `signalr-events.model.ts:64-66` both state the ids are stripped.

**Fix:** Look the interface property up by CLR name: `(propertyInfo.AttributeProvider as MemberInfo)?.Name` instead of `propertyInfo.Name` (or compare with `StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase`). Add a unit test in `Wbskt.Host.Tests` that serializes one event of each context interface with the hub's options and asserts the ids are absent: nothing tests this modifier today (no test mentions `SignalRPrivate`, and the E2E suite has no hub scenario).

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the lookup uses the JSON name, that the interfaces are the only place the attribute sits for `WorkspaceId`/`ClientId`/`PolicyId`, and that no test covers it; that SignalR's default payload naming policy is camelCase and that `JsonPropertyInfo.Name` is already converted when a modifier runs is from knowledge of the framework, not from a run)

### 8. A presence repair stamped with the management host's clock overrides a newer real connect

P2 · Auth and management · size S · #737

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/SocketHostStartedHandler.cs:36-49; Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/SocketHostPresenceSnapshotHandler.cs:36-41; Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Client_ResetPresenceBy_HostId.sql:13-20; Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Client_UpdatePresence.sql:15-43; Wbskt.Gateway.Core/HostedServices/StartupPresenceResetPublisher.cs:12-13, 26-49

**Scenario:** A gateway container restarts in place (crash, `docker restart`, a VM reboot), so it keeps its host id. It publishes `SocketHostStartedEvent`; its clients reconnect within seconds and each publishes `ClientConnectedEvent` stamped with the gateway's time `Tc`. If the management host handles the started event after `Tc` (it was restarting too, as after a VM reboot; its queue was behind; or the gateway's first publish failed and the 5 s retry in `StartupPresenceResetPublisher` sent it late), then `Client_ResetPresenceBy_HostId` clears every client with `ConnectedHostId = @HostId` with no regard for when it connected, and the handler publishes a `ClientDisconnectedEvent` for each whose `CreatedAtUtc` is the management host's "now" `Tr > Tc`, naming the same host. Whatever order the three messages are consumed in, the client ends offline: connect then reset clears it; reset, connect, synthetic disconnect passes `LastActivityAt <= Tr AND ConnectedHostId = @HostId` and clears it; reset, synthetic disconnect, connect drops the connect as stale (`LastActivityAt < Tc` is false). The client is connected and the database says it is not, so the console shows it offline, commands sent to it are queued instead of delivered, and offline triggers fire. Nothing repairs it: the presence snapshot only ever clears presence, never sets it. It lasts until the client's next reconnect. The snapshot handler has the same shape for a client that reconnects between the snapshot being taken and its synthetic disconnect being consumed (it guards the clear with `TakenAtUtc` but stamps the disconnect with "now").

**Fix:** Carry the host's own time through. In `Client_ResetPresenceBy_HostId` take `@StartedAt` (the started event's `CreatedAtUtc`) and clear only rows with `ConnectedAt < @StartedAt`; publish the synthetic disconnect with `CreatedAtUtc` set to that start time, and the snapshot handler's with `TakenAtUtc`, so a connect the gateway saw later always wins in `Client_UpdatePresence`. Add an integration test: connect consumed before the started event, and after it.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the procedure has no time predicate, that `BaseEvent.CreatedAtUtc` defaults to `DateTime.UtcNow` at construction, the three orderings against `Client_UpdatePresence`, and that the management queues are durable so a started event waits for a restarting management host; how often the first started publish fails and is retried 5 s later is inferred from the comment in the publisher, not seen)

### 9. SMTP sends have no timeout, so a relay that stalls stops both mail drains

P2 · Auth and management · size XS · #738

**Where:** Wbskt.Infrastructure/Email/SmtpEmailSender.cs:28-55; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Email/OutboundMailDispatcher.cs:51-63; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingNoticeMailSender.cs:17-21, 44-56

**Scenario:** `SmtpClient.SendMailAsync(message, ct)` is the only bound on a send, and `ct` is the host's stopping token. `SmtpClient.Timeout` applies to the synchronous `Send` only. A relay that accepts the connection and then stops answering (or a firewall that drops packets) leaves the send waiting until the TCP stack gives up, which can be minutes or never. `OutboundMailDispatcher` drains sequentially, so every verification, reset and invitation mail queues behind that one send; its comment (lines 11-14) says the retry ladder is "bounded so a permanently broken relay cannot wedge the drain loop", which holds only for a relay that fails fast. In `BillingNoticeMailSender` the batch of 50 is leased for 5 minutes at take time and sent one by one, so a slow relay (more than about 6 s a mail) runs the later mails past their lease; a second auth instance then takes and sends them too.

**Fix:** Bound each send in `SmtpEmailSender`: a linked `CancellationTokenSource` with `CancelAfter` (30 s, configurable in `EmailOptions`) and translate its cancellation into a `TimeoutException` so both callers treat it as a failed attempt. In `BillingNoticeMailSender`, stop the batch when the lease is nearly spent (or take fewer at a time).

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed no timeout is set anywhere on the path and both drains are sequential; that `SmtpClient.Timeout` does not apply to `SendMailAsync` is from the framework's documentation, not from a run; the double send needs a second auth replica, which production does not run today)

### 10. Deleting a workspace takes its month's credits and messages off the tenant's meters

P2 · Credits and usage · size M · #739

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Retire.sql:78`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Functions/TenantWorkspaces.sql:9-13`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:140-153`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/WorkspaceMessageUsage_GetAccounts.sql:17-37`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/BusinessUsage_GetBy_Month.sql:27-32`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Create.sql:23`

**Scenario:** A Maker tenant spends its 25,000 credits in workspace B and its starts are refused with 50033. The owner deletes B. `Workspace_Retire` deletes B's row in `dbo.WorkspaceAccounts`. `Run_Start` sums the ledger only over `dbo.TenantWorkspaces`, which is the starting workspace plus the tenant's rows in `dbo.WorkspaceAccounts`, so B's ledger rows no longer count and `@PlanLeft` is 25,000 again. `Workspace_Create` counts only workspaces that still exist, so the owner creates another and repeats. A Free tenant does the same with its one workspace (2,000 credits each time). The message meter behaves the same way: `WorkspaceMessageUsage_GetAccounts` pools only over `dbo.WorkspaceAccounts`, so a throttled account is unthrottled by deleting the workspace that carried the traffic. With packs it is worse: `@CreditsDrawn` still comes from `dbo.TenantCreditMonths`, so `@PlanLeft = allowance - (ledger - drawn)` can come out above the allowance. `dbo.TenantCreditMonths.Posted` keeps the deleted workspace's credits, so the grant draw and `Run_Start` now disagree about the same month. The Business overage report loses the deleted workspace's messages for the same reason. No test covers a retired workspace's usage (`WorkspaceRetirementIntegrationTests`, `CreditLedgerIntegrationTests`).

**Fix:** Keep the account row on retire and mark it (`RetiredAt`), and let `dbo.TenantWorkspaces`, `WorkspaceMessageUsage_GetAccounts` and `BusinessUsage_GetBy_Month` keep counting a retired workspace's month. For credits alone a smaller change also works: when `@TenantId` is known, `Run_Start` reads the month's posted total from `dbo.TenantCreditMonths.Posted`, which is already kept per tenant, in place of the ledger sum.

**Confidence:** high (the second read confirmed the delete at line 78, that every month read joins through `dbo.WorkspaceAccounts`, and that nothing else records a retired workspace's tenant; `Docs/Billing.md:71-73` calls the removed copy harmless because it thinks only of live clients and runs)

### 11. Credits of a run that has ended count nowhere until the poster moves them, so caps and the allowance are passed

P2 · Credits and usage · size M · #740

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:145-151`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Functions/WorkspaceCreditsSpent.sql:14-19`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/RunCredits_Post.sql:30-43`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_DeleteRetired.sql:53-76`; `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Configuration/WorkflowEngineOptions.cs:34-35`

**Scenario:** Both reads of "spent" add unposted credits only for runs whose status is Running, Failing or Cancelling. A run that ends takes its unposted credits out of both sums until `CreditUsagePoster` next runs (every 10 s). Workspace cap 100: run A spends 100 inside one poster interval and ends; `PostedCredits` is still 0. Run B starts a second later: `WorkspaceCreditsSpent` is 0, `WorkspaceCreditsReserved` is 0, `@CapLeft` is 100, so B gets the whole cap and spends another 100. The workspace has spent 200 against a cap `Docs/Billing.md:146` and `Docs/Billing.Policies.md:57-58` say is held exactly. The tenant allowance has the same hole: a trigger firing 50 short runs a second at 3 credits each puts 1,500 credits between two posts that no start sees. The bound in `Docs/Billing.md:116-118` ("what was left times the runs going at once") does not cover this, since the runs are not going at once. Second part: the poster only looks back `CreditPostEndedWindow` (1 hour) for ended runs. If it does not post for longer than that (engine down, or the procedure failing every tick), a run that ended before the gap keeps its unposted credits until `Run_DeleteRetired` removes it 7 to 90 days later, and posts them on that day (`@Today`), in a later month's ledger, allowance and overage report.

**Fix:** Count ended runs with unposted credits in both reads, the way `RunCredits_Post` finds them (the `CompletedAt >= @EndedSince` branch over `IX_Runs_CompletedAt`), and drop the one-hour window from the poster by marking a counter row that has unposted credits (a bit set by `RunCounters_TryCharge` and `Run_Start`, cleared by the post, with a filtered index) so nothing is left for `Run_DeleteRetired` to post late.

**Confidence:** high for the hole (the second read found no post in `Run_SetTerminal.sql` and no test for an ended run's credits; `CreditLedgerIntegrationTests` only has `Credits_a_running_run_has_not_posted_yet_still_count`); medium for how often the one-hour window is missed, which depends on outages

### 12. A credit notice at 80% or 100% is lost for good when the broker is down for eight seconds, or the engine stops after the post commits

P2 · Credits and usage · size M · #741

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/HostedServices/CreditUsagePoster.cs:49-50,87-111`; `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Entities/WorkspaceCreditPost.cs:22-53`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/RunCredits_Post.sql:87-104`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_DeleteRetired.sql:78-92`

**Scenario:** The crossing is worked out from one post's step (`before < threshold && after >= threshold`), after the post has committed, and nothing in the database records that a notice is owed. Three ways it never goes out: (1) the broker refuses the publish four times in about 7.5 s (`WarningRetryDelays`), the poster logs "the warning is lost", and the next post starts from above the threshold; (2) the engine stops or loses its connection between the commit at `RunCredits_Post.sql:104` and the publish; (3) the step that crosses is posted by `Run_DeleteRetired` (documented in `Docs/Billing.md:441-442`). For a Business tenant the 80% and 100% notices are the promise in `Docs/Billing.Policies.md:52-53` that "the invoice is never a surprise". The message notices do not have this problem: they are claimed in `dbo.WorkspaceMessageLimitNotices` and given back when the publish fails (`MessageAllowanceMonitor.cs:92-104`).

**Fix:** Record what was announced beside the totals (a `NotifiedPercent` on `dbo.TenantCreditMonths` and on a per-workspace month row for caps) and have the poster announce whatever is past a threshold and not yet recorded, marking it only after the publish succeeds, as the message monitor does.

**Confidence:** high (the second read confirmed the result set is the only carrier of the crossing and that `_brokerDown` skips retries for every later warning in the outage)

### 13. The Business overage report leaves out a tenant whose contract ended and the messages of a deleted workspace

P2 · Credits and usage · size M · #742

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/BusinessUsage_GetBy_Month.sql:14-23,27-32,38`; `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Billing/BusinessOverageService.cs:31-49`; `Docs/Billing.md:128-133,393-398`

**Scenario:** The report lists tenants whose latest account copy says Business now, and nothing stores a month's result. A Business plan is an operator plan with `endsAt`; when the date passes, `SubscriptionReviewWorker` moves the tenant off Business by itself. A contract that ends on 30 November is on Free or a subscription plan on 1 December, so the November report run in December has no row for it and its overage is never invoiced. `Docs/Billing.md:133` warns only about a contract an operator changes by hand. Messages are summed through `dbo.WorkspaceAccounts`, so a workspace deleted before the report is run takes its messages out of the bill (see the first finding). Credits come from `dbo.TenantCreditMonths` and are not affected by a delete. What the report counts against what `Docs/Billing.Policies.md` bills: credits are `Posted - Drawn - CreditsPerMonth` at $0.50 per 1,000, with packs drawn first once the plan has covered its allowance, which matches the policy; messages are inbound plus outbound past `MessagesPerMonth` at $1 per million, which matches. Differences: credits are billed in the month they were posted, not consumed (a few seconds' worth at a month edge, more after a poster outage); the allowance is the contract as it stands when the report runs, for the whole month (documented); a revoked grant's drawn credits stay "drawn", so credits paid for by a pack that was later charged back are not billed.

**Fix:** Close each month once: a job on the 1st (or the first report call for a past month) writes each tenant that was on Business at month end, its allowances and its totals to a table, and the report reads that table for past months. Sum messages by tenant without going through live account copies.

**Confidence:** high for the report's query; medium for the contract-end path (the automatic move at `endsAt` is taken from `Docs/Billing.md:393-398`; `SubscriptionReviewWorker` belongs to the billing lane and was not read)

### 14. A cap set while runs are going is not held for those runs, and their budgets block every new start

P2 · Credits and usage · size M · #743

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/WorkspaceCreditCap_Set.sql:12-32`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/RunCounters_TryCharge.sql:17-25`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Functions/WorkspaceCreditsReserved.sql:9-18`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:187-192`

**Scenario:** A run started with no cap has `BudgetCeiling` NULL and its whole budget (up to `DefaultCreditBudgetPerRun`, 10,000, or the tenant's remaining credits). The owner sees a loop burning credits and sets a cap of 1,000. `WorkspaceCreditCap_Set` only writes the cap. The looping run's charges take the fast path in `RunCounters_TryCharge`, which checks `r.CreditBudget` alone, so it carries on to its own budget, far past the cap. Meanwhile `WorkspaceCreditsReserved` counts that run's unspent budget (thousands), so `@CapLeft` is negative and every other start in the workspace is refused with 50034 "has used its monthly credit cap" although the response to the PUT said `capReached: false`. A run parked on a wait for days does the same to a workspace that sets its first cap. `Docs/Billing.Policies.md:57-58` promises runs that would pass the cap are refused or stopped.

**Fix:** In `WorkspaceCreditCap_Set`, under the `credit-cap:{workspaceId}` lock, turn the workspace's running root runs into leased ones: set `BudgetCeiling` to the current budget and cut `CreditBudget` to what the run has spent plus one lease, so the next charge goes through the cap check.

**Confidence:** high (the second read confirmed `WorkspaceCreditCap_Set` touches no run, and that the fast path has no cap check; no test sets a cap with a run going)

### 15. `used`, `limit` and `topUp` in the usage response stop adding up to the refusal point once a pack is drawn

P2 · Credits and usage · size S · #744

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Billing/WorkspaceUsageService.cs:44-49`; `Models/Wbskt.Management.Models/Usage.cs:23-32`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/WorkspaceUsage_GetSummary.sql:63,72`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:152-163`; `Docs/API.Endpoints.md:607`; `Docs/Billing.md:295`

**Scenario:** Maker (25,000) with one 10,000 pack, 30,000 credits posted. The draw has taken 5,000 from the pack as the credits were posted, so the grant balance is 5,000. The response says `used: 30000, limit: 25000, topUp: 5000`. The contract ("a run is refused once `used` reaches `limit + topUp`") says the account is out. `Run_Start` computes `25000 - (30000 - 5000) + 5000 = 5000` left and starts the run. The gap is the month's `Drawn`, which the response does not carry: `used` includes drawn credits while `topUp` no longer does. The comment at `Usage.cs:27-28` still says the overage "comes out of `TopUp` when the month closes", which was the settlement that #680 replaced. The console's usage page (wbskt/Wbskt.Console#151) is not built yet and would be built to this contract, showing every account that has touched a pack as out of credits. On Business the same sentence is also wrong by the overage multiple (the API doc mentions it, the model comment does not).

**Fix:** Return what is left as `Run_Start` computes it (a `remaining` field from the same expression, with the month's `Drawn` read from `dbo.TenantCreditMonths`), or add `drawn` and state the rule as `used >= limit + drawn + topUp`. Correct the comment in `Usage.cs` and the two docs.

**Confidence:** high (worked through with the numbers above against both procedures)

### 16. The monthly credit allowance can be passed by the number of runs going at once times what was left

P2 · Credits and usage · size L · #745

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:159-166`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/RunCounters_TryCharge.sql:17-25`; `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/RunStarter.cs:293-294`; `Docs/Billing.md:116-119`

**Scenario:** This is documented as soft, so the finding is its size, not that it exists. Each start is given a budget of everything the tenant has left, less only what other runs have already consumed. No race is needed: a Free tenant on the 1st starts 20 looping runs (its active-run limit) a few seconds apart; each gets a budget near 2,000 and each is only stopped at its own budget, so the month can reach about 40,000 credits against an allowance of 2,000. On Maker it is 100 runs times up to 25,000 (a workflow's own `CreditBudget` can be set above the 10,000 default), on Pro and Team 200 runs. One $5 pack raises "what is left" for every one of those budgets. `Docs/Billing.Policies.md:49-50` allows runs already going to finish, which is what makes the overrun free of charge on every plan but Business.

**Fix:** Lease the tenant's remainder the way a capped workspace's is leased: give a start a slice (for example a twentieth of what is left, or its start cost if more) and extend it in `RunCounters_TryCharge` from what the tenant still has. A smaller step is to cap a start's budget at a share of what is left divided by the plan's `ActiveRuns`.

**Confidence:** high for the arithmetic (read in `Run_Start` and the fast path of `RunCounters_TryCharge`); the owner may accept it as designed

### 17. A duplicate subscription's refund is claimed before it is asked for, and a timeout or restart leaves the claim behind so it is never refunded

P2 · Billing and pricing · size S · #746

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/TenantBillingSync.cs:212-229 (claim at 214, catch at 224), Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Program.cs:81 (30 s HttpClient timeout), Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/TenantSubscription_ClaimDuplicateRefund.sql:21-24

**Scenario:** Two checkouts are paid, the second subscription is marked a duplicate and cancelled. `RefundDuplicateAsync` sets `DuplicateRefundPaymentId` and then calls Dodo. If that call times out (HttpClient throws `TaskCanceledException`, which is an `OperationCanceledException`), or Dodo's own 30 s delivery timeout aborts the request, or the host restarts between the claim and the call, the `catch ... when (ex is not OperationCanceledException)` at line 224 does not run and the claim is not released. Every redelivery then sees `DuplicateRefundPaymentId` set (line 60) and skips the refund. The customer has paid twice, the log says "cancelling it and refunding its payment", and nothing asks again.

**Fix:** Release the claim on every exception (a plain `catch` with `CancellationToken.None`), or drop the claim-first design: the payment is read fresh on every delivery, so ask for the refund whenever the duplicate's payment is still `Succeeded` and not `Reversed`, and treat Dodo's "already refunded" answer as done.

**Confidence:** high (second read: the filter excludes `OperationCanceledException`, `TaskCanceledException` derives from it, and no other path clears the column; the test at Tests/Wbskt.Host.Tests/AuthHost/BillingPaymentsTests.cs:235 only covers a refused refund)

### 18. Nothing reads the provider back, so a webhook Dodo gives up on leaves a paid tenant on Free, a paid pack ungranted, or a lapsed subscription on its paid plan for good

P2 · Billing and pricing · size S · #747

**Where:** Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/TenantSubscriptions_GetToReview.sql:16-22, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/SubscriptionReviewWorker.cs:29, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/IPaymentGateway.cs:10-55 (no list call), Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingWebhookService.cs:22-25

**Scenario:** Dodo retries a delivery 8 times over about 27.5 hours and then stops (its docs; a replay after that is manual, from its dashboard). If the auth host or its database is down, the route is wrong, or the signing secret is rotated on one side only for longer than that, the events are gone. Three states are then never repaired: (a) a first `payment.succeeded` or `subscription.active` never arrives, so the customer has paid and stays on Free; (b) a credit pack payment never arrives, so no row is written and the hourly grant reconcile (which reads `dbo.TenantCreditPurchases`) has nothing to repair; (c) a stored subscription stays `active` after its renewal failed or it was cancelled, and `TenantSubscriptions_GetToReview` lists only `past_due`, `on_hold`, `cancelled`, `expired`, `failed` and `paused` rows, so an `active` row whose `CurrentPeriodEnd` passed weeks ago is never read again and the tenant keeps the paid plan without paying. A 401 on the signature is also not counted on `wbskt_auth_billing_webhooks_total`, so neither alert sees a wrong secret.

**Fix:** Smallest first: add `OR (s.Status = N'active' AND s.CurrentPeriodEnd < DATEADD(DAY, -1, @Now))` to the review query so a stale active row is re-read from Dodo, and count refused signatures on the metric with an alert. Then a daily reconcile that lists the provider's payments and subscriptions changed since the last run and feeds each id to `TenantBillingSync`.

**Confidence:** high for the code (second read: the review query, the worker and the gateway interface have no other path; the retry schedule is from Dodo's webhook docs); the outage itself is an operational assumption

### 19. One Dodo customer per payer email means a tenant's billing portal opens another tenant's subscriptions and invoices

P2 · Billing and pricing · size M · #748

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/DodoPaymentGateway.cs:27-31, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/TenantBillingService.cs:102-105 and 245-252, Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/TenantBillingCustomer_FindBy_TenantId.sql:10-20

**Scenario:** A checkout is created with `customer: { email, name }` of the signed-in user and nothing else. Dodo's checkout docs say "By default email is used to find an existing customer to attach the session to", so a person who pays for tenant A (their company) and tenant B (their own) is one Dodo customer. `PortalAsync` opens the portal for the customer id stored on the tenant's subscription row, for any member with `billing.manage`. A second billing admin of tenant A opens the portal and is signed in as that shared customer: they see tenant B's subscriptions, invoices and saved card, and can cancel tenant B's subscription. The same holds after the payer leaves tenant A: its remaining admins still open the ex-member's customer.

**Fix:** Keep one provider customer per tenant: create the customer once (or pass `always_create_new_customer` on a tenant's first checkout), store its id against the tenant, and send `customer: { customer_id }` on later checkouts. Rated P2 rather than P1 because it needs one person paying for two tenants and a second billing admin; it is still one tenant's billing data and control reaching another.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the request body and the portal lookup; that Dodo matches customers by email is quoted from its API reference; that the portal lists every subscription of the customer is how a customer portal works but was not exercised)

### 20. A refunded or charged-back subscription payment drops the plan only if the payment lists the plan's product, which Dodo documents for one-time payments only

P2 · Billing and pricing · size S · #749

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/TenantBillingSync.cs:53-57, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/DodoPaymentGateway.cs:95, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/DodoWire.cs:56, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/SubscriptionPlan.cs:72-73, Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/Tables/TenantSubscriptions.sql:8-9

**Scenario:** `SyncPaymentAsync` sets `refundedThrough` only when one of `payment.Lines` is a plan product, and takes the interval from that line. `Lines` is Dodo's `product_cart`, which its API reference types as nullable and describes as "List of products purchased in a one-time payment". If a subscription payment comes back with no cart, a full refund or a lost chargeback sets nothing: the subscription stays `active`, and the tenant keeps the paid plan (for the rest of a year on an annual plan) after getting the money back. Billing.Policies.md says the tenant "drops to Free at once". The unit test at Tests/Wbskt.Host.Tests/AuthHost/BillingPaymentsTests.cs:400-411 passes a hand-built payment with a plan line, so it does not show what Dodo sends. Two smaller edges on the same lines: the table comment says `RefundedThrough` is "the period end at the time" of the refund, but the code stores `paidAt` plus one interval plus a day, so a refund of a mid-period proration charge withholds a plan whose base period was paid for, and a charge made on the 29th to 31st of January gets a through-date of 1 March that a provider period ending 2 or 3 March would already pass.

**Fix:** Take the interval from the stored subscription's own product (`offer` in `SyncStoredSubscriptionAsync`, which is already resolved there) and pass only "this payment was reversed, made at T". Add a gateway test with a recorded test-mode subscription payment.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the code path and the docs wording; whether a live subscription payment's `product_cart` is null could not be checked without a Dodo test account)

P2 · Billing and pricing · size S · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#178
