### 22. Prices exist only in Dodo's dashboard and in prose, so a swapped product id sells one plan's limits at another's price and nothing notices

P2 · Billing and pricing · size M · #750

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingOptions.cs:17-20 and 38-52, deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:225-233, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/PaymentGatewayCheck.cs:13-39, Models/Wbskt.Auth.Models/TenantBilling.cs:52-54

**Scenario:** No price, currency or interval is in the code. `Billing:Payments` maps a plan and interval to a Dodo product id, and the pack to a product id plus `Credits: 10000`. If `DODO_PRODUCT_MAKER_MONTHLY` is set to the Pro product, or the yearly variable to the monthly product, or the pack product is priced at something other than $5 in the dashboard, a customer pays one price and gets another plan's limits, and `PaymentGatewayCheck` (which only probes the API key) logs "accepts this deployment's API key". The billing read returns offers as `(plan, interval)` and `(name, credits)` with no amount, so the console screens (Console #155) will have to hardcode the prices a fourth time beside Billing.md, the site and the dashboard. This is the one place the "one set of prices" rule cannot be checked from the repos: (a) and (b) in the table below hold no price at all.

**Fix:** Put the price list in the catalog (`PlanCode`, interval, USD amount; the pack's amount), have `PaymentGatewayCheck` read each configured product from Dodo at start and log Critical when its price, currency or billing period differs, and return the amount on `PlanOffer` and `CreditPackOffer`. Also make `FindPlan` and `FindCreditPack` skip empty product ids.

**Confidence:** high (second read: no call to Dodo's products API anywhere in Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host; the response records carry no amount)

### 23. One Team account at its uncharged 2x message headroom is past the single-VM database trigger, which the #698 gate counts at 1x

P2 · Billing and pricing · size S · #751

**Where:** Wbskt.Infrastructure/Billing/PlanCatalog.cs:22, Wbskt.Infrastructure/Billing/MessageAllowance.cs:16 and 26-30, deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:556 (`MSSQL_PID` Express), Docs/Billing.md:238-241

**Scenario:** Team includes 200 M messages a month (77 msg/s sustained) and is throttled only at twice that, with nothing charged. The capacity estimate's optimised figure is about 35 MB of traffic rows per sustained msg/s at the 2-day retention now in force, and the hosting plan says to leave the single VM when either database nears 8 GB (Express stops at 10 GB) or traffic passes about 300 msg/s. One Team account at its allowance is 2.6 GB of traffic rows before its 30 days of raw readings; at the 2x it is allowed for $99 it is 154 msg/s and 5.3 GB, which with readings is the whole database. #698 records the gate as "one full Team account fills about half ... move off Express before a second Team account fills up", which is the 1x figure. Four Team accounts at their allowance (or two at 2x) also reach the 300 msg/s trigger while paying $396 (or $198) a month against the $500 to $900 the next stack costs.

**Fix:** Decide one of: throttle Team at a lower multiple (`Billing:PaidMessageThrottleMultiple` is one number for Maker, Pro and Team; make it per plan), restate the #698 gate at 2x, or add an alert on database size and sustained msg/s so the move is triggered by a number rather than by counting Team accounts.

**Confidence:** medium (arithmetic from the two capacity files and the catalog; the capacity figures are a model, not a measurement, and #448, the load tester that would measure them, is still open)

### 24. Raw webhook bodies are kept for ever, with the payer's name, email, address and card last four

P2 · Billing and pricing · size S · #660 (already filed)

**Where:** Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/Tables/BillingWebhookEvents.sql:10, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingWebhookService.cs:27, site: app/src/app/pages/privacy/privacy.html:18 and :33

**Scenario:** Every delivery's body is stored in `Payload` and nothing deletes it; the table has no tenant column, so deleting an account leaves its payer's details behind. The privacy page, reworked on 11 November, says card details "go straight to our payment provider and never reach us" and that a deleted account "is removed from the live service straight away". A Dodo payment event carries the customer's name, email, billing address and the card's network and last four digits.

**Fix:** As #660 proposes (blank `Payload` a day after processing, delete processed rows after 30 days). It has no milestone; it belongs in M3 now that the privacy page makes the promise.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed nothing prunes the table; the card fields in the payload are from Dodo's payment schema as remembered, not re-read here)

### 25. POST /api/v1/tenants lets one account create any number of Free tenants

P2 · Tenants and access · size S · #752

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Controllers/ManagementController.cs:39-49; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantService.cs:70-80; Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/Tenant_Create.sql (no count, no limit); Wbskt.Infrastructure/Billing/PlanCatalog.cs:19 (Free is 1 workspace, 1 member, 20 clients, 2,000 credits a month)

**Scenario:** A signed-in user loops `POST /api/v1/tenants {"name":"t1"}`, `{"name":"t2"}` and so on. Each call creates a tenant on Free with its own default workspace and its own Free allowance. Nothing counts tenants per user and nothing but the per-user limiter (600 requests a minute) slows it, so one sign-up (one proof of work, one mail address) gets as many Free workspaces, client pools and monthly credit allowances as it asks for, and the Free plan's "1 workspace" limit means nothing. There is also no endpoint to delete a tenant, so the rows stay until the account is deleted.

**Fix:** Cap the tenants one user may create (for example one unpaid tenant per user: refuse in `Tenant_Create` under a lock on the user row when the caller already administers a Free tenant they are the only member of), and return a 409 with its own code.

**Confidence:** high (second read of the controller, the service and the procedure; no limit anywhere on the path). That an unsubscribed tenant resolves to Free comes from `TenantPlanService` and `WorkspaceAccountSync`.

### 26. Registration tells whether an email is registered when sent with a username known to be taken

P2 · Tenants and access · size S · #753

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/RegistrationService.cs:196-199 and 298-349 (`HandleRegistrationConflictAsync` looks the email up first and answers 204 when it exists, otherwise 409 `AUTH_USERNAME_CONFLICT`); Docs/Auth.Host.E2E.Scenarios.md:123-124 (`AUTH_REG_04`, `AUTH_REG_05`)

**Scenario:** The design answers a duplicate email exactly like success so the endpoint cannot be used to test addresses. The combination defeats it. Request sequence: (1) the attacker registers `probe1` with their own address; (2) for each target address, solves one challenge and sends `POST /api/auth/register {"username":"probe1","email":"<target>","password":"<any 12+ characters, not breached>", "challenge":..., "solution":...}`. The insert always fails on a unique index. If the target address has an account the handler finds it and answers **204**; if it has none the handler answers **409** `AUTH_USERNAME_CONFLICT`. No account is ever created, so a probe leaves nothing behind, and a registered target also receives an "account exists" mail per cooldown window. The cost is one proof of work per probe and the per-IP limit of 10 a minute.

**Fix:** Decide the username first. On the unique violation look up the username; when another account holds it, answer 409 whatever the email is (a registered address plus a taken username then reads the same as an unregistered one). Only when the username is free does the email decide, and that case answers 204.

**Confidence:** high (second read: there is no username pre-check before the insert at lines 107-116, and the handler's order is email first). Not run.

### 27. Ending one session, or changing the password, signs the user out everywhere as soon as the ended device refreshes

P2 · Tenants and access · size S · #754

**Where:** Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/RefreshToken_Rotate.sql:49-58 (any token with `Revoked IS NOT NULL` is a replay and revokes every live token of the user); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/SignInService.cs:204-221 (also revokes the access tokens and raises `RefreshTokenReplay`), 305-320 (`RevokeSessionAsync`), 288-297 (`LogoutAllAsync`); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/PasswordService.cs:103-112; Docs/Auth.Host.E2E.Scenarios.md:192 (`AUTH_RT_18` records it as "a separate decision")

**Scenario:** A token retired by a deliberate revoke looks the same as one retired by rotation. Request sequence: (1) the user is signed in on laptop A and phone B; (2) on A, `DELETE /api/auth/sessions/{B's session}` (or `POST /api/auth/change-password`, which revokes the other sessions and returns a new pair to A); (3) B's console still holds its refresh cookie, gets a 401 on its next call and sends `POST /api/auth/refresh` (the interceptor does this on every 401, Wbskt.Console src/app/core/api/api.interceptor.ts:17-18); (4) the procedure reads B's token as revoked, answers `Replayed`, revokes A's fresh token too, the service revokes all access tokens and publishes a `RefreshTokenReplay` security alert. The user who ended one session, or just changed their password, is signed out on the device they did it from, and operations get a false theft alert each time. The same follows `logout-all`. The console already guards the two-tab race (src/app/core/auth/token-refresh.service.ts:11-30), so this is the remaining false positive.

**Fix:** Treat as a replay only a token that rotation retired: read `ReplacedByTokenHash` in the first select of `RefreshToken_Rotate` and branch `@Revoked IS NOT NULL AND @ReplacedByTokenHash IS NOT NULL` to `Replayed`; a revoked token with no replacement returns a new outcome that answers `AUTH_TOKEN_INACTIVE` and changes nothing. Update `AUTH_RT_18` and the tests that pin the current behaviour (Tests/Wbskt.E2E.FeatureTests/Scenarios/Auth/TokenRotationTests.cs, AccountSecurityTests.cs).

**Confidence:** high (second read of the procedure, the three revoking paths and the console interceptor). Not run.

### 28. Account deletion cancels the paid subscription before it knows the deletion will be allowed

P2 · Tenants and access · size S · #755

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/AccountDeletionService.cs:83-99 (cancel at line 83, delete at line 95); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/User_Delete.sql:48-60 (THROW 50018, sole administrator of a shared tenant) and 68-78 (THROW 50019, tenants changed); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/DodoPaymentGateway.cs:113-133 (the cancel is immediate, status `cancelled`)

**Scenario:** A user pays for Maker on their personal tenant (they are its only member) and is also the only administrator of a second tenant that has other members. They send `POST /api/auth/account/delete {"password":"..."}`. `CancelSubscriptionsAsync` cancels the personal tenant's subscription at the provider, then `User_Delete` refuses with 50018 and the API answers 409 `ACCOUNT_SOLE_ADMINISTRATOR`. The account still exists, the tenant still exists, and the subscription they paid for is gone. The same happens on the 50019 race.

**Fix:** Check the blocking conditions before touching the provider: a read-only procedure (or a dry-run flag on `User_Delete`) that returns the 50018 tenants, called before `CancelSubscriptionsAsync`. The window that remains (a member joins between the check and the delete) is then the rare race, not the common path.

**Confidence:** high on the order (second read of the service and the procedure). Medium on what the provider does with the paid period after an immediate cancel; that is the billing lane's to confirm.

### 29. The test that requires a permission on every workspace-scoped action matches no route since /api/v1, and the auth host has no such test

P2 · Tenants and access · size S · #756

**Where:** Tests/Wbskt.Host.Tests/ManagementHost/ManagementEndpointPermissionTests.cs:18 (`WorkspaceRoutePrefix = "api/workspaces/{workspaceRef"`) and 98-107; every management controller's route is `api/v1/workspaces/{workspaceRef:guid}/...` (for example Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Controllers/ClientsController.cs:17); Wbskt.Infrastructure/Authorization/ScopedPermissionFilter.cs (an action with no attribute passes the filter untouched); Docs/Coding.Conventions.md "Enforced by a test"

**Scenario:** `Every_workspace_scoped_action_declares_its_permissions` keeps only actions whose route starts with `api/workspaces/{workspaceRef`. Since the API moved under `/api/v1` (commit 2f09ed0; the unversioned paths are now a rewrite in `UnversionedApiAliasMiddleware`, not routes) no route starts with that, so the list is always empty and the test always passes. `Each_action_requires_exactly_the_permissions_it_always_has` looks only at actions that already have a requirement, and `Only_actions_with_requirements_bind_the_workspace_id` only catches an action that takes `[FromWorkspace]`. So a new action such as `[HttpGet("export")] Export(Guid workspaceRef)` with no `[RequiresPermission]` passes all three, and because the filter skips actions without the attribute it is open to any signed-in user of any tenant, with no membership check. Today every management action does carry the attribute (checked by script over both hosts' controllers), so nothing is open now; the guard the conventions rely on is what is gone. The auth host has no equivalent at all: nothing in `Tests/` pins which `api/v1/tenants/{tenantRef}` action needs which `[RequiresTenantPermission]`, or that `WorkspacesController`'s scoped actions carry `[RequiresPermission]`; the three deliberate exceptions (`ManagementController.LeaveTenant` line 350, `WorkspacesController.GetMyPermissions` line 73, `WorkspacesController.CreateWorkspace` line 54) are not written down anywhere a test reads.

**Fix:** Match on the route containing `/workspaces/{workspaceRef` (or build the prefix from the route constant) and add an assertion that the filtered list is not empty. Add the same test for the auth host: every action whose route contains `{tenantRef` or `{workspaceRef` declares a requirement, with a pinned table and a named allow-list of the three exceptions.

**Confidence:** high (second read of the test, the controller routes and the alias middleware; the prefix cannot match). Not run, since there is no .NET SDK here.

### 30. users.manage alone can hand out any role, through an invitation or a group

P2 · Tenants and access · size S · #757

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Controllers/ManagementController.cs:87-88 (`POST {tenantRef}/invitations` needs `users.manage`), 276-277 (`POST members/{userRef}/groups/{groupRef}` needs `users.manage`), against 292-293 and 308-309 (assigning a role or a permission needs `roles.manage`); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantService.cs:189-198 (any role of the tenant is accepted); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/TenantInvitation_Accept.sql:75-80 (the role is granted tenant-wide); Docs/AccessControl.md:205-208 (states the split)

**Scenario:** The design separates "who is in the tenant" (`users.*`) from "who may do what" (`roles.*`). A tenant gives a team lead a custom role holding `users.read` and `users.manage` so they can invite people. Request sequence: (1) the lead reads a role reference, from `GET /api/v1/tenants/{t}/invitations` (needs only `users.read`, and each row carries `roleRef`) or from the console; (2) `POST /api/v1/tenants/{t}/invitations {"email":"lead+2@example.com","roleRef":"<Admin role>"}`, which answers with the raw token; (3) registers that address with `invitationToken`, or accepts it from an account on that address. The second account is now tenant-wide Admin: `roles.manage`, `billing.manage` and everything else. The shorter path, when any group already carries a role, is `POST /api/v1/tenants/{t}/members/{own userRef}/groups/{groupRef}`. Nothing compares the role being granted with what the caller holds; there are no levels at all, so the same holder can also remove or suspend any other member, the tenant's creator included (only the last-administrator guard stops them).

**Fix:** Require `roles.manage` as well when an invitation names a role (`roleRef` not null) and for adding a member to a group that carries roles; or, more simply, refuse to grant by invitation or group any permission the caller does not hold tenant-wide. State the rule in AccessControl.md.

**Confidence:** high on the attributes and the grant (second read of the controller, the service and the procedure). The default roles do not expose it (only Admin holds `users.manage`), so it needs a custom role; that is the inferred part.

### 31. The MQTT gateway accepts a client id in any GUID spelling but can only disconnect the canonical one, so a revoked or rotated client can stay connected and keep receiving the client's commands

P2 · Gateways · size XS · #758

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Services/MqttConnectAuthenticator.cs:38, 74` (`Guid.TryParse` and a `Guid` compare), `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Infrastructure/MqttConnection.cs:46` (`MqttClientId = ClientRefId.ToString("D")`), `:167-186` (`GetSessionAsync(MqttClientId)` and `DisconnectClientAsync(MqttClientId, …)`), `:155-165` (the catch-all that hides the miss)

**Scenario:** A client connects with its `clientRefId` in upper case, in braces or without dashes as the MQTT client id (`Guid.TryParse` takes all of them; Swift's `uuidString` and Windows tools print upper case). MQTTnet keys its sessions and clients by the raw string. Every close the gateway asks for (revoked, secret rotated, throttled, superseded from another host, too big) goes through `DisconnectAsync`, which looks the session up by the lower-case form, gets `InvalidOperationException` ("Session … not found"), and `TryDisconnectAsync` swallows it. The Wbskt session ends and the client is announced offline, but no DISCONNECT is sent and the TCP connection stays up, still subscribed to `wbskt/v1/{id}/down`. Two consequences. (1) Security: rotate a leaked secret, the holder of the old one is "closed" but stays subscribed; when the real device signs in with the new secret (a different client-id spelling, so MQTTnet sees no takeover), every command the gateway injects on the down topic is delivered to both. The same holds for a client revoked and later approved again. (2) An honest client with an upper-case id that is throttled or superseded is never told, and silently loses everything it publishes from then on.

**Fix:** In `MqttConnectAuthenticator`, refuse (0x85) a client id that is not exactly `clientRefId.ToString("D")`, as the doc already says ("in its usual form"). Or keep the id the client connected with on `MqttConnection` and use it for the lookup and the disconnect. Add a test for an upper-case id.

**Confidence:** medium. Second read confirmed the gateway code and, on MQTTnet `master`, that `GetSessionStatus` throws for an unknown id, that `_clients` and the session store are keyed by `connectPacket.ClientId` as sent, and that an injected message goes to every session subscribed to the topic. Not run.

### 32. The WebSocket gateway never sends close code 1001 when it shuts down: cancelling the receive aborts the socket first

P2 · Gateways · size S · #759

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.WebSocket.Host/Services/SocketHandler.cs:80-83` (the receive token is linked to `ApplicationStopping`), `Clients/Wbskt.Protocol/WebSocketMessageReader.cs:49`, `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/ClientSessions.cs:142-145, 158`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.WebSocket.Host/Infrastructure/ClientConnection.cs:49-63, 102-113`; `Docs/Client.Protocol.WebSocket.md:61`, `Docs/Client.Protocol.md:222, 235`

**Scenario:** Every deploy stops gateway-ws. `ApplicationStopping` cancels the token each `ReceiveAsync` is waiting on. In .NET a cancelled `ManagedWebSocket.ReceiveAsync` aborts and disposes the socket (`cancellationToken.Register(s => ((ManagedWebSocket)s).Abort(), this)`; `Abort()` is "forcibly tear down connection"), so its state is `Aborted` before the session's `finally` runs. `CloseTransportAsync(ServerStopping)` then calls `CloseOutputAsync`, whose guard (`State is Open or CloseReceived`) is false, so no Close frame goes out. Every client sees an abnormal drop (1006) and reconnects on the 0 to 10 s schedule, not the 0 to 30 s spread the protocol gives 1001 for exactly this case, so a restart's reconnect wave is three times denser than designed, and the SDKs' 1001 branch never runs against the real gateway. The MQTT gateway does it right (it sends DISCONNECT 0x8B before the server stops, and `MqttGatewayTests.A_gateway_that_stops_tells_its_clients_it_is_shutting_down` proves it over loopback). The WebSocket tests only prove the mapping against a mocked socket whose state is always `Open` (`ClientConnectionTests.cs:34-41, 56`) and a fake connection (`ClientSessionTests.cs:187-196`).

**Fix:** Do not link `ApplicationStopping` into the receive token. Register on it to call `connection.RequestCloseAsync(DisconnectReason.ServerStopping, …)`, which sends Close 1001 under the send lock and already cuts the connection after `CloseGrace` if the client does not answer. Add a loopback test with a real `WebSocket.CreateFromStream` pair (or an E2E that stops the host) asserting the client reads 1001.

**Confidence:** high on the code path (second read: nothing else closes connections at shutdown; `ServerStopping` is only ever produced in `ClientSessions.cs:144`), high on the .NET behaviour (read in `ManagedWebSocket.cs`, dotnet/runtime `release/10.0`, lines 433-439 and 782). Not run.

### 33. After a broker outage longer than two minutes every connected client is shown offline until it reconnects; the presence snapshot repairs only the other direction

P2 · Gateways · size S · #760

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/StalePresenceSweeper.cs:59` and `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Client_ExpirePresence.sql:17-30` (clears every client of a host with no heartbeat for `HostStaleAfterSeconds`, 120 by default, `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Models/SocketHostPresence.cs:16`); `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/HostedServices/HeartbeatPublisher.cs:49-57` (a failed beat is only logged); `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/ClientSessions.cs:132-136` and `Services/Inbound/InboundMessageDispatcher.cs:69-99` (sessions now survive a broker outage, `dd70bc8`); `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/SocketHostPresenceSnapshotHandler.cs:36` and `Client_ReconcilePresenceBy_HostId.sql:18-28` (the snapshot only clears)

**Scenario:** RabbitMQ is down, partitioned from the gateways, or blocking publishers on a memory or disk alarm for more than two minutes, while management and SQL stay up. No heartbeat is recorded, so the sweeper marks every client held by every gateway offline. The gateways keep all their sessions (that is what `dd70bc8` fixed for the 2 Nov review's M8). When the broker is back the heartbeats resume, but nothing marks the clients connected again: a connect is announced once, at session start, and the five-minute snapshot only clears rows the host no longer holds. Until each device happens to reconnect (for a stable device that can be days, or the next deploy), the console shows it offline while its messages keep arriving, every command to it is queued rather than sent and can expire after 24 hours unsent, and anything that reads presence from the database sees an outage that is not there. The same end state follows when `ClientPresenceHandler` dead-letters a `ClientConnectedEvent` after its five tries (a SQL outage over about a minute while clients reconnect).

**Fix:** Let the snapshot repair both ways: in `Client_ReconcilePresenceBy_HostId`, also set `IsConnected = 1, ConnectedHostId = @HostId, ConnectedAt = @TakenAt` for snapshot refs the database shows offline with `LastActivityAt < @TakenAt - margin`, return them, and have the handler announce `ClientConnectedEvent` (which also delivers their queued commands). Send one snapshot as soon as a heartbeat succeeds after a failed one, not only every five minutes.

**Confidence:** medium. Second read confirmed the sweeper's predicate, the snapshot procedure and that no code path re-announces a held connection. Inferred: how long a real broker outage lasts; not run.

### 34. A revoke, delete or secret rotation that a gateway misses is never applied to the connection already open

P2 · Gateways · size S · #761

**Where:** `Events/Wbskt.EventBus.RabbitMQ/EventBusExtensions.cs:68-80` (per-instance queues are `Durable = false`, `AutoDelete = true`), `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Handlers/ClientAccessHandlers.cs:19-78` (the only place an open connection is closed for lost access), `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/ClientSessions.cs:75-89, 126-130` (the Redis cutoff is read at admission only), `Wbskt.Infrastructure/Security/ClientTokenCutoffs.cs:33-41`

**Scenario:** A gateway's connection to RabbitMQ drops for a few seconds (a broker restart, a network blip). Its auto-delete queue goes with it. A user revokes or deletes a client, or rotates a leaked secret, in that window; management writes the Redis cutoff and publishes the event, which reaches an exchange with no queue bound for that gateway and is discarded. Since `dd70bc8` the client's session survives the blip. The Redis cutoff stops the client from connecting again, but nothing ever looks at it for a connection that is already open, and an open connection is not tied to its token's expiry either. The revoked device stays connected indefinitely: its messages keep reaching the event log and keep starting workflow runs, and a client whose secret was rotated keeps receiving commands. `ClientTokenCutoffs` says the cutoff "is what makes revocation hold regardless"; that is true for new connections only.

**Fix:** Re-check held connections against the cutoffs on a timer. Keep `IssuedAtUtc` on the connection and, in the `PingSampler` round or beside the presence snapshot, ask `IClientAccessGate.IsRefusedAsync` for each held client and close the refused ones as `Revoked`. Cheaper still: close a connection when its token's lifetime has passed and let the client reconnect with a fresh one (the SDKs already hold one), which bounds the gap at an hour.

**Confidence:** medium. Second read confirmed the queue settings, that the three access handlers are the only callers of `RevokeAsync`/`RevokeIssuedBeforeAsync`, and that `IsRefusedAsync` is called from `AdmitAsync` only. Inferred: that MassTransit publishes in the gap are dropped rather than buffered for an unbound queue (standard RabbitMQ behaviour for a fanout exchange with no binding). The window is seconds wide, so this is rare; what makes it P2 is that it is never repaired.

### 35. .NET client: an OnConnected or OnSuperseded handler that throws leaves a dead connection or stops reconnecting for good

P2 · Client libraries · size S · #762

**Where:** src/Wbskt/Internal/SocketClient.cs:73-75; src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:144-150; src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:211-214

**Scenario:** (a) The app's `OnConnected` handler throws. `SocketClient.ConnectAsync` raises `OnConnected` on line 73, before it starts the receive loop on line 75, and `WbsktClient.HandleConnected` raises the app's event on line 146 before it announces capabilities on line 149. The exception leaves `ConnectAsync` with the socket open, no receive loop and no `capabilities` sent. The monitor then sees `IsConnected == true` and idles, so the client never answers `sys.ping`, never receives a command and never reconnects. (b) The app's `OnSuperseded` handler throws. It is raised on WbsktClient.cs:213 outside any try block inside `MonitorReconnectionAsync`, which runs as a discarded `Task.Run`, so the reconnect loop ends silently and the client stays offline until the process restarts. The 2 Nov H3 fix isolated only `OnMessageReceived` (SocketClient.cs:216-230). Python guards `on_connected` (client.py:283-286) but not `on_disconnected` (client.py:260-261), where a throw turns a clean close into a failed attempt and loses the close code, so a 4003 close is retried on the short schedule instead of after 30 to 60 s.

**Fix:** Wrap each lifecycle event invocation in try/catch that forwards to `OnHandlerError` (Python: log it, as for `on_connected`), and start the receive loop and the capabilities announcement before raising `OnConnected`.

**Confidence:** high for the control flow (second read found no guard around either invocation); what the socket does after (a) is inferred, .NET was not run.

### 36. .NET message handlers cannot be async without an exception ending the process

P2 · Client libraries · size S · #763

**Where:** src/Wbskt/IWbsktClient.cs:7 and src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:28 (`event Action<string, object?, string?>`); src/Wbskt/Internal/SocketClient.cs:214-230 (the isolation); src/Wbskt/Internal/SocketClient.cs:175 (payload is a boxed `JsonElement`)

**Scenario:** Every way a handler can answer is async (`SendAsync`, `ReportStateAsync`), so the natural handler is `wbskt.OnMessageReceived += async (type, payload, id) => { ...; await wbskt.SendAsync(...); }`. Against an `Action` event that lambda is `async void`. The try/catch in `DispatchLoopAsync` only covers the code before the first `await`; anything thrown after it (a `JsonException` reading the payload, `InvalidOperationException` from `SendAsync` with buffering off) is rethrown on the thread pool and terminates the process, and `OnHandlerError` never sees it. Ordering is lost too: the loop hands over the next message while the first handler is still awaiting. Python takes coroutines and awaits them one at a time (client.py:320-324, 454-459). The event shape, with `object?` for a payload that is always a `JsonElement` and no place for `expiresAt`, is on NuGet as 0.9.0-beta.1 and becomes permanent at 1.0.

**Fix:** Before 1.0, add an awaited handler (`Func<WbsktMessage, Task>` set through a property or `OnMessage(...)`), await it inside the dispatch loop's try block, and say in the README that the payload is a `JsonElement`.

**Confidence:** medium (async void behaviour is the runtime's documented behaviour; not run here).

### 37. .NET and Python keep credentials at the same path in two different formats, so each wipes the other's

P2 · Client libraries · size S · #764

**Where:** wbskt.dotnet/src/Wbskt/FileClientStorage.cs:23-33, 43, 58-59 (`{"RefId","Secret"}` in `<LocalApplicationData>/wbskt/<name>.json`); Wbskt.Python/src/wbskt/storage.py:28-31, 36, 43, 73-78 (`{"clientRefId","secret"}` in `<data dir>/wbskt/<name>.json`)

**Scenario:** On Linux both resolve to `$XDG_DATA_HOME` or `~/.local/share`, on Windows both to `%LOCALAPPDATA%`, and both READMEs and the site use the name `greenhouse-sensor`. A user who tries one SDK and then the other on the same machine with the same name, or moves a Pi script from Python to .NET, hits this: the second SDK cannot find its own keys in the first one's file, treats it as "no credentials", registers again with the PIN and overwrites the file. The first SDK does the same on its next start. Each swap is a new client in the console and one more against the policy's limit, and the old client is orphaned. With no PIN configured the second SDK fails with `RegistrationRequired` although valid credentials are on disk.

**Fix:** Use one file format in both (the protocol's own names, `clientRefId` and `secret`), with the old .NET keys read as a fallback. Both packages are 0.9 betas, so this is the cheap moment.

**Confidence:** high (both load paths read: a missing key returns "no credentials" in each, FileClientStorage.cs:62-65 and storage.py:37-38; both defaults resolve to the same folder).

### 38. .NET client does not refuse a message over 64 KB, and a large one can block the buffer for ever

P2 · Client libraries · size XS · #765

**Where:** src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:249-257 (`Check(type, null)`: the payload is never measured), 259-287, 303-314, 324-328; src/Wbskt/Internal/SocketClient.cs:78-103

**Scenario:** The protocol's checklist says an SDK "never sends ... a message over 64 KB" (Client.Protocol.md section 6). Python raises `ValueError` (client.py:164-165) and the ESP32 library returns `false` (WbsktClient.cpp:551-553). The .NET client serialises and sends whatever it is given, through `SendAsync` and `ReportStateAsync` alike. The gateway ends the connection with 1009 and the client reconnects. If the send had completed before the close, that message is gone and the client carries on. If the message is large enough that the gateway closes while it is still being written, `SendAsync` throws, `FlushOutboundAsync` keeps it at the head for the next connection (WbsktClient.cs:308-312), and every reconnect resends it and is closed again, with every later message stuck behind it. The conformance suite has no scenario for the size limit, so nothing catches the difference.

**Fix:** Measure the serialised message in `EnqueueAsync` and throw `ArgumentException` over `ClientProtocol.MaxMessageBytes`, as the other two do. Add a conformance scenario (a 70 KB `send` expects `rejected`).

**Confidence:** high that the check is missing (read twice; `ClientMessageRules.MaxPayloadChars` exists but is passed `null`). Medium for the stuck buffer: it depends on the gateway closing mid-write, which was not run.

### 39. .NET client gives the app no way to see why it is not connecting

P2 · Client libraries · size S · #766

**Where:** src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:79-98 and 219-227 (every failure after the first attempt is caught and retried); nothing under src/Wbskt logs, traces or raises an error event apart from `OnHandlerError`

**Scenario:** A wrong PIN, a full policy, approval still pending, a revoked client, a wrong URL and a TLS failure all look the same to a .NET app: `StartAsync` returns normally and `OnConnected` never fires. `RegistrationRequiredException` is thrown only from `StartAsync`; if it arises later (the stored credentials were rejected with `CLIENT_UNAUTHORIZED` and there is no PIN) the monitor swallows it and retries every minute for ever. Python logs each of these on the `wbskt` logger and stops with `RegistrationRequired` through `wait_closed()` (client.py:204-207, 213, 362). The ESP32 library logs them (WbsktClient.cpp:174, 255, 287) and its README has a troubleshooting table keyed on those lines (README.md:349-353). The .NET README has none, because there is nothing to show.

**Fix:** Add an `OnConnectFailed(Exception)` event (or accept an optional `ILogger`), and surface the terminal "no credentials and no PIN" case through it. Settle the name before 1.0.

**Confidence:** high (grep of src/ for ILogger, Console, Debug, Trace and EventSource finds nothing).

### 40. PlatformIO install line names a version the publish workflow has never run for

P2 · Client libraries · size XS · #767

**Where:** README.md:59-70 (`wbskt/Wbskt@^0.9.0-beta.1`, added in 5cfea33); .github/workflows/release.yml (added in db57228); tag `v0.9.0-beta.1` points at 982c544, which is older than both

**Scenario:** The only tag and GitHub release, `v0.9.0-beta.1`, were made on 9 Nov at 982c544, before `release.yml` existed, and a tag push only runs workflows present at the tagged commit. GitHub Actions lists zero runs of `release.yml` in wbskt/Wbskt.Arduino (the Python and .NET release workflows each list one successful run). So unless someone ran `pio pkg publish` by hand, the PlatformIO registry has no `wbskt/Wbskt`, and a user who pastes the README's `lib_deps` gets an unresolved dependency. Pushing the same tag again cannot publish it.

**Fix:** Bump the three version files to 0.9.0-beta.2 and tag it (CONTRIBUTING.md already uses that as its example), then check the registry page. Until then give the Git URL form for PlatformIO in the README.

**Confidence:** medium (GitHub side checked: no workflow run, tag older than the workflow. The registry was not queried, so a manual publish cannot be ruled out).

### 41. Get-started sketch cannot be uploaded as written, and the page promises boards the library does not support

P2 · Client libraries · size XS · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#178

**Where:** app/src/app/pages/get-started/get-started.html:9, 28-32, 34-54

**Scenario:** Step 4 says "Paste this in, add your PIN and Wi-Fi details, and upload it". The sketch calls `readTemperature()` (line 52), which exists nowhere, so it fails to compile; the library's own README uses the built-in `temperatureRead()` (Wbskt.Arduino/README.md:28). Step 3 does not mention the partition scheme, and the README says Wi-Fi plus TLS need about 1.3 MB against the default 1.2 MB app partition (README.md:72-74; CI builds only with `min_spiffs`, .github/workflows/ci.yml:65-69), so on default board settings the sketch does not fit even once it compiles. "You'll need: An ESP32, or an Arduino with Wi-Fi" (line 9) is wrong for an UNO R4 WiFi, Nano 33 IoT or MKR: `library.properties` says `architectures=esp32`, the README says other boards are not supported (README.md:76-77), and more boards are open as post-launch work (#568, #570, #571).

**Fix:** Use `temperatureRead()` in the sketch, add "Tools, Partition Scheme, Minimal SPIFFS" (or the PlatformIO line) to step 3, and change the list item to "An ESP32 (ESP32, S3 or C3)".

**Confidence:** high for the undefined function and the board claim; medium for the partition size, which is the README's and CI's statement and was not built here.

### 42. A wake that is in flight when the engine stops is lost, and the branch is failed BOOKMARK_LOST ten minutes later

P2 · Workflow engine · size S · #768

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Bookmark_ClaimDue.sql` (the claim deletes the row; its own comment calls the loss an accepted trade-off), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BookmarkResumer.cs:103` and `:127`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/HostedServices/BookmarkScheduler.cs:31` and `:55`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/ChannelRunDispatcher.cs:12`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BranchLoop.cs:237-243`, `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Branch_GetRunning.sql:22`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/HostedServices/RunRecoveryService.cs:39`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/StrandedBranchFailer.cs:34`

**Scenario:** A branch is parked on a delay, a wait for a signal, a command acknowledgement or a retry back-off. The timer or the inbound event claims the bookmark by deleting it and puts a request on the in-memory channel; the branch row stays `Waiting` until its resumed node moves it on. The engine stops in that window: a deploy, a crash, or the leader stopping itself after a failed lease renewal. The channel is gone, the bookmark is gone, and recovery only re-dispatches `Active` branches (`Branch_GetRunning` filters on `Status = N'Active'`). For an inbound wake the `inbound-event` claim may already be `Succeeded`, so a broker redelivery is dropped as a duplicate. Nothing ever wakes the branch. `BookmarkOrphanGc` then sees a `Waiting` branch with no bookmark on two passes and `StrandedBranchFailer` fails it with `BOOKMARK_LOST`. The user sees a step that failed for no reason of theirs, on an ordinary deploy as well as on a crash. With a backlog on the channel (50 workers, 64 bookmarks claimed per tick) the window is as long as the queue.

**Fix:** In `RunRecoveryService.RecoverAsync`, after the `Active` branches, also dispatch every `Waiting` branch that has no bookmark (the query `Branch_GetWaitingWithoutBookmark` already exists) with reason `Recovered`. The parking executors already resume correctly from persisted state: they read the park marker, the deadline, `__wake`, `PendingTakePort` and `__retry_attempt`, and `KeepWaitingOnAsync` parks again when the wake is not theirs. The orphan GC then only fails branches that recovery could not explain.

**Confidence:** high for the sequence (second read confirmed the delete-as-claim, the in-memory channel, the `Active`-only recovery query and the GC failing the branch). The length of the window in production is inferred; no numbers are available.

### 43. A sub-workflow step can wait forever on a child that has already finished

P2 · Workflow engine · size M · #769

**Where:** `Wbskt.Workflow/NodeExecutors/Controls/SubWorkflowNodeExecutor.cs:105` and `:129` (child started in the first visit, then the park, with no TTL and no `AfterParked`), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BookmarkResumer.cs:49-53`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/RunFinalizer.cs:82-87` and `:93-118`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/InboundAdapters/EventBusRunCompletedPublisher.cs:24-31`, `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_GetStuck.sql` (skips runs holding a bookmark), `Docs/Workflow.Engine/Design.Patterns.md:113`

**Scenario:** Two ways lead to the same stuck parent. (a) `RunFinalizer.FinalizeAsync` sets the child's terminal status first (line 82) and calls the completion hook last (line 118). Between them it appends to the journal, drains the trigger queue and awaits a publish on the real RabbitMQ bus (the engine host registers `AddRabbitMqEventBus`, not the queued bus the other hosts use for after-the-fact events). If any of those throws, or the process dies there, the hook never runs, and a second `FinalizeAsync` returns at line 83 because the run is already terminal. (b) The child is started inside the parent's first visit, before the parent's bookmark exists. If the child reaches its end before the parent's park is written, the `child-completed` event finds no bookmark and is dropped without a claim (`BookmarkResumer.cs:49-53`). In both cases the parent holds a bookmark with no expiry, so the reaper skips it (`Run_GetStuck`), the orphan GC sees nothing wrong, and the parent run stays `Running` and counts against the plan's active-run ceiling until someone cancels it. Case (a) also leaves events queued behind the finished run (Queue concurrency policy) undrained, since the drain is only called from the finalizer.

**Fix:** Give the parked parent a safety net that does not depend on the one event: have `SubWorkflowNodeExecutor` pass an `AfterParked` that reads the child run and, when it is already terminal, raises the `child-completed` event itself; and add the same check to a periodic sweep (or to recovery) for `child-completed` bookmarks whose child run is terminal. In `RunFinalizer`, call the completion hook and the queue drain before the broker publish, and catch and log a failed publish, as the comment on `QueuedEventBus` argues for events that record work already committed.

**Confidence:** high for (a): the order of the calls, the early return and the reaper's skip were confirmed on the second read; how MassTransit behaves with the broker down (throw or wait) is inferred. Medium for (b): the order is confirmed and contradicts Design.Patterns.md ("start the child in `AfterParked` so a fast reply always finds the bookmark"), but the child needs many more SQL round trips than the parent's park, so the race needs the parent to stall.

### 44. An event whose first delivery died mid-dispatch is dropped as a duplicate when the broker redelivers it

P2 · Workflow engine · size S · #770

**Where:** `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BookmarkResumer.cs:69-84` (only a `Failed` claim is reclaimed; `Pending` under another token is "dropped as idempotent"), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BookmarkResumer.cs:141` (`MarkFailedAsync(..., ct)` on the request's own token), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/TriggerDispatcher.cs:430-432` (same rule for the per-registration claim), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/TriggerDispatcher.cs:179` and `:195` (the same release done correctly with `CancellationToken.None`), `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/IdempotencyKey_ReclaimFailed.sql`, `Docs/Workflow.Engine/Design.Patterns.md` ("A claim marked Failed can be reclaimed, so a crash mid-dispatch does not lose the event")

**Scenario:** A client message, webhook callback or command outcome is being dispatched: its `inbound-event:{id}` claim (and, for a trigger, its `trigger-start:` claim) is `Pending`. The engine process is killed, or the host stops and the consumer's token is cancelled. In the second case the catch at `BookmarkResumer.cs:139-141` tries to mark the claim `Failed` with the already cancelled token, which throws before the write, so the claim stays `Pending`. The message was never acknowledged, so RabbitMQ delivers it again to the restarted (or new leader) engine. The new attempt reads a `Pending` claim under another token, cannot tell a live attempt from a dead one, and settles the event as `Idempotent`. No run starts and no bookmark is resumed; the only trace is an information log line. The claim row lives until the idempotency retention deletes it, so every later redelivery is dropped too.

**Fix:** Pass `CancellationToken.None` at `BookmarkResumer.cs:141`, as `TriggerDispatcher` already does. Make a `Pending` claim reclaimable once it is clearly dead: `IdempotencyKey_ReclaimFailed` (or a sibling) also takes a `Pending` row whose `CreatedAt` is older than a short bound (a minute is far longer than any dispatch), or older than this leader's start. Correct the sentence in Design.Patterns.md until then.

**Confidence:** high that a `Pending` claim is never reclaimed and that line 141 uses the cancellable token (both re-read). Medium on how often a consumer is cancelled mid-dispatch on a graceful stop; that depends on MassTransit's stop behaviour, which was not run.

### 45. Run now that the plan refuses answers 409 "dropped by the concurrency policy", and a busy workspace answers 500

P2 · Workflow engine · size S · #771

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/Controllers/InboundDispatchProjection.cs:32-34` (`Refused => InboundOutcome.Dropped`), `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Clients/WorkflowEngineClient.cs:24` and `:36`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Workflow/WorkflowRunService.cs:62-65`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Channels/ManualChannel.cs:34` (manual triggers always register `AllowParallel`), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/TriggerDispatcher.cs:339-364` and `:330`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Workflow/WorkflowTestRunService.cs:117-153` (the test-run path that reports the same refusals properly), `Docs/API.Endpoints.md:462`

**Scenario:** A workspace is at its plan's active-run ceiling, out of the month's credits, or at its workspace credit cap, and someone calls `POST /workflows/{workflowRef}/runs`. The engine refuses the start (`TriggerDispatchOutcome.Refused`), the projection folds that into `Dropped`, and the management host answers 409 `RUN_DROPPED_BY_CONCURRENCY_POLICY` with "already has an active run and its concurrency policy discarded this request". A manual trigger is always `AllowParallel`, so for this endpoint `Dropped` can only ever mean a plan refusal, and the message is always wrong. An API caller is told to wait for a run that does not exist instead of being told the plan limit was reached. When the workspace lock times out, `RunStartBusyException` leaves the engine as a 500, `EnsureSuccessStatusCode` throws in the management host, and the caller gets a 500, where the test-run endpoint answers 503 `WORKSPACE_BUSY` for the same condition. The console shows "Failed to start the run" for all of these (`workflow-page.service.ts:163-167`, `workflow-explorer.ts:253-259`).

**Fix:** Add a `Refused` value (with the reason) to `InboundOutcome` and `StartRunOutcome`, and map it in `WorkflowRunService` to the codes the test-run path already uses (`PLAN_RUN_LIMIT_REACHED`, `PLAN_CREDIT_LIMIT_REACHED`, `WORKSPACE_CREDIT_CAP_REACHED`). Catch `RunStartBusyException` in the engine's manual controller and answer 503, mapped to `Error.Unavailable("WORKSPACE_BUSY")`. Update API.Endpoints.md:462. The console then shows `err.error.detail`.

**Confidence:** high (the whole chain was re-read end to end; only the 500 for the busy case is inferred from the absence of a catch in `InboundManualController` and `InboundHub`).

### 46. The designer's Test run button starts a live run of the published version, and drafts stay in the browser

P2 · Workflow engine · size M · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#170

**Where:** `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/workflow-tab/services/workflow-page.service.ts:151-169` and `:227`, `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/workflow-tab/components/top-bar/workflow-top-bar.html:72-75`, `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/services/workflow-draft.service.ts` (local storage only), `src/app/core/constants` `API_ENDPOINTS.WORKFLOWS` (no `draft`, `drafts` or `test-runs` entry); backend side `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Controllers/Workflow/WorkflowDraftsController.cs:31-77`

**Scenario:** Someone edits a workflow in the designer and presses "Test run". The console calls `POST /workflows/{ref}/runs`, which starts a live run of the published version, not of the edited graph on screen. The run is not marked as a test (`isTest` is false), counts in the live history and statistics, and the button is offered only when the definition has a "Run by hand" trigger. The backend shipped server-side drafts (`GET/PUT/DELETE {workflowRef}/draft`, `POST draft/publish`) and test runs of a draft from any trigger (`POST {workflowRef}/test-runs`) in this range (wbskt/Wbskt#598, #599, both closed); the console calls none of them. Drafts live in one browser's local storage, so a draft is invisible on another device and to `GET workflows`' `draft` field, and a workflow that was never published cannot be test-run at all.

**Fix:** Point Test run at `POST {workflowRef}/test-runs` with `source: "draft"` when the tab has unpublished changes (saving the draft to the server first), and move `WorkflowDraftService` onto the draft endpoints with its `revision` check. Until then, rename the button to "Run now".

**Confidence:** high (the console's endpoint table and both call sites were re-read; no call to the draft or test-run routes exists in `src/app`).

**Also found by the console and site lane:** Workflow drafts live only in the browser although the server now stores drafts. A user edits a workflow on a laptop, does not publish, and opens the console on another machine or clears site data: the draft is gone. A second member cannot see that an unpublished draft exists. The list's `draft` flag from the server is never read.

### 47. A step the designer has just dropped fails Validate, Publish and a draft save as a 400 from the serializer, not as a validation problem

P2 · Workflow engine · size S · #772

**Where:** `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Models/Triggers/ClientTriggerConfig.cs` (`required` `ClientRef`, `Type`), `ClientPresenceTriggerConfig.cs` (`required` `ClientRef`), `WebhookTriggerConfig.cs` (`required` `Path`), `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Models/Nodes/Controls/LogicGateConfig.cs` (`required` `Condition`), `ForkConfig.cs` (`required` `Branches`), `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Models/Nodes/Triggers/ClientTriggerNode.cs:9` (`required` `Config`); `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Controllers/Workflow/WorkflowsController.cs:42-46` and `WorkflowDraftsController.cs:46-51` (typed bodies); the rules that can never be reached for a missing key: `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Validation/WorkflowValidator.Steps.cs:151-156` (`FORK_BRANCHES_MISSING`) and `:186-190` (`LOGIC_CONDITION_MISSING`), `WorkflowValidator.cs:447-454` (`INVALID_CLIENT_REF`); console `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/utils/node/node-factory.util.ts` (`DEFAULT_CONFIG` and `createNode`)

**Scenario:** In the designer a new "Client message" trigger starts as `{ type: '*', holdSeconds: 0, concurrencyPolicy: 'Queue' }`, a new "Client online / offline" trigger as `{ state, forSeconds, concurrencyPolicy }`, a new "Webhook received" trigger without `path`, and a new If or "Split into paths" step as `{}`. Each of those lacks a property the engine's record marks `required`. The user presses Validate to see what is still missing. The request body is bound as a typed `WorkflowDefinition`, System.Text.Json refuses the missing required member, and the answer is a 400 for the whole request with no step and no rule code, so the designer cannot outline the step that needs attention. The validator has rules written for exactly these cases, but they only run when the key is present and empty. The same binding guards `PUT {workflowRef}/draft`, so once the console moves its drafts to the server a half-built graph cannot be saved at all, which is what a draft is for.

**Fix:** Drop `required` from the config members the validator already checks (give them empty defaults, as `SendClientMessageConfig`, `EmailConfig` and `ForEachConfig` do) and add the missing rules (`TRIGGER_CLIENT_REQUIRED`, `WEBHOOK_PATH_MISSING`). The designer can also seed the keys in `DEFAULT_CONFIG`, but the server should not depend on it.

**Confidence:** medium. The `required` members, the designer's defaults and the typed bodies were re-read. That the request fails in model binding follows from how System.Text.Json treats `required`; it was not run, and the designer may write the missing key as soon as a panel is opened.

### 48. Readings of a deleted workspace are never swept

P2 · Database · size S · #773

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/ClientReadings_Compact.sql:31-41` (the cutoffs come from `dbo.Clients JOIN dbo.WorkspaceAccounts`), `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Retire.sql:34-38,78` (clients are revoked, not deleted, and the workspace's `WorkspaceAccounts` row is deleted)

**Scenario:** A workspace with clients that reported readings is deleted. `Workspace_Retire` revokes its clients (they stay in `dbo.Clients`, so they are never queued in `dbo.DeletedClientReadings`) and deletes its `WorkspaceAccounts` row. From then on `ClientReadings_Compact` builds `#Cutoffs` only for clients whose workspace has an account row, so those clients have no cutoff, and step 3 only clears clients listed in `DeletedClientReadings`. Their rows in `dbo.ClientReadings` and `dbo.ClientReadingsHourly` stay forever. Before this range `ClientReadings_DeleteBefore` deleted every reading past one global age, so this arrived with e475fee. `Run_DeleteRetired` does not have the gap: a workspace with no account falls back to `@CutoffUtc` (`Run_DeleteRetired.sql:40-44`).

**Fix:** In `Workspace_Retire`, insert the workspace's client ids into `dbo.DeletedClientReadings` (the sweep then clears them in batches and drops the rows), or give a client with no account row a fallback cutoff in `ClientReadings_Compact` as `Run_DeleteRetired` does. The credits lane proposes keeping the account row on retire and marking it; if that lands, the readings are swept on the plan's days again, but a deleted workspace's readings should still be cleared outright.

**Confidence:** high (second read of both procedures and of `ClientReadingRetentionService.cs:63-91`, which calls nothing else; the periodic account sync reads only workspaces that still exist in the auth database, so it does not put the row back)

**Scenario:** A Business tenant (`RunHistoryDays` 90), or any tenant with a `runHistoryDays` override above 30, opens a run that finished 40 days ago. `Run_DeleteRetired` still keeps the run, as the plan says. But the first pass of `HistoryRetentionGc` calls `HistoryEvent_DeleteForRetiredRuns` with one global cutoff (`HistoryRetentionWindow`, 30 days) and no plan, and it deletes every `HistoryEvents` row that is not Warn or Error for runs finished before that. The run page shows the run with only its warnings and errors, or no steps at all for a run that succeeded. Billing.md says a finished run "with its branches and history" is kept for the plan's days.

**Fix:** Drop the Info pass (run retention already deletes a run's history with the run, per plan), or pass the same per-plan retention table to it so its cutoff is never earlier than the workspace's run cutoff.

**Confidence:** high (second read: the procedure takes no plan input; the provider and the worker pass only the two global cutoffs; Business is 90 days in `BusinessContract.cs:39`). Not run.

### 52. The live connection never comes back once automatic reconnect gives up, or when the first start fails

P2 · Console and site · size S · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#173

**Where:** `src/app/core/signalr/signalr.service.ts:70` (`withAutomaticReconnect()` with the default schedule), `:88` (`onclose` only sets the state), `src/app/features/workspace/workspace-shell.ts:61` (start is called once, with no `catch` and no retry)

**Scenario:** The management host restarts for a deploy and is away for longer than SignalR's default retries (0, 2, 10 and 30 seconds, then it stops). `onclose` fires, the status bar says "Offline", and nothing calls `startConnection` again: `startConnection` has exactly one caller, the shell's `ngOnInit`. Every console tab open at that moment stays without live updates until the user reloads the page, while lists keep showing the last patched presence. The same happens when the very first `start()` fails (automatic reconnect does not cover the initial start), and that rejection is unhandled.

**Fix:** Pass a retry policy that never gives up (`withAutomaticReconnect({ nextRetryDelayInMilliseconds: ctx => Math.min(30000, 1000 * 2 ** ctx.previousRetryCount) })`), and in `onclose` and on a failed first start schedule `startConnection()` again with the same back-off, rejoining `_joined` and `_watched` as `onreconnected` does. Refresh `WorkspaceDataService` after a reconnect, since events were missed.

**Confidence:** high for the missing restart (one caller, read twice); the default retry schedule is the library's documented behaviour, not re-read from its source here

**Scenario:** A workspace with 60 workflows opens the Workflows explorer: one list request, then 60 definition requests fired at once, each returning a full graph, only to pick an icon and collect webhook paths. Any publish, deprecate, reinstate or delete by anyone re-reads the list (`onLifecycle`). A failed definition read is swallowed (`error: () => undefined`) and that row keeps a blank icon for the session. The list response already says what starts each workflow.

**Fix:** Add `triggerKinds?: string[]` (and `draft`) to `WorkflowSummary` in `workflow-api.model.ts`, derive the icon from `triggerKinds[0]`, and read definitions only when a webhook path check is needed (the duplicate-path warning), not for every row.

**Confidence:** high (both sides read; the comment at `workflow.service.ts:65` still says "The list doesn't return it")

### 54. Run detail shows only the first 200 history events and counts them as the whole run

P2 · Console and site · size S · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#175

**Where:** `src/app/core/api/workflows/workflow-api.service.ts:140-148` (`getRunHistory`, `limit = 200`, no cursor parameter); `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/workflow-tab/components/runs/run-details/run-details.ts:166-168` (one read, `response.items` kept, `nextCursor` ignored, a failure only goes to `console.error`); `run-details.html:79` (`{{ historyRows().length }} events`)

**Scenario:** A run with a for-each over 150 items writes several history events per item. The run detail panel asks for history once with `limit=200`, gets the oldest 200 events and a `nextCursor`, and drops the cursor. The timeline stops part of the way through the run, the header says "200 events", and the step that failed at the end is not in the list. Nothing says the list was cut.

**Fix:** Read history through a `CursorPager` with `wbsktInfiniteScroll` as the other lists do (or loop on `nextCursor`), and show "200+" while `hasMore` is true.

### 55. Any refresh drops rows past the first page: a roster scrolled past 200 falls back to 200

P2 · Console and site · size S · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#176

**Scenario:** A workspace has 450 clients. The user scrolls the explorer to row 400 (pages one and two loaded) and renames a client there, or a new device enrolls anywhere in the workspace. `refresh()` replaces the roster with the first 200 rows; the rows the user was looking at disappear, the scroll position jumps, an open policy tab's derived client counts change, and `PolicyPageService` (`policy-page.service.ts:113`) sets its policy to `null` if that policy sat on a later page. More than 200 pending clients are never listed at all.

**Fix:** Make `CursorPager.refresh()` re-read as many rows as are loaded (walk the cursor until `items.length` reaches the previous length), and stop refetching for changes the hub already patches (next finding). Give the pending pager a cursor.

**Confidence:** high for the code path (read twice); medium on how often it is hit, since Free allows 20 clients and only larger plans reach 200

### 59. Public Traefik routers exclude `/api/internal` with a case-sensitive matcher, and the hosts route case-insensitively

P2 · Infra and CI · size S · #775

**Where:** deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:256 (auth router), deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:324 (management router); Wbskt.Infrastructure/InternalRoutes.cs:9-11; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Controllers/InternalWorkspacesController.cs:18-21; Wbskt.Infrastructure/Authorization/InternalApiKeyAttribute.cs:28-48; Tests/Wbskt.Host.Tests/Architecture/PublicRoutingTests.cs:23-33

**Scenario:** Both public routers carry `!PathPrefix(`/api/internal`)`. Traefik's `PathPrefix` is a plain string prefix test on the request path, so it is case-sensitive; ASP.NET Core route matching is not. A request to `https://auth.<DOMAIN>/API/internal/workspaces/resolve` (or `/Api/Internal/...`) does not match the exclusion, the router admits it, and Kestrel routes it to `InternalWorkspacesController`. A signed-in user gets back `ResolvedWorkspaceResponse` with the internal integer workspace id, and `/API/internal/users/me` gives the internal user id, both of which the conventions say never leave the backend. The key-guarded routes (`PUT /api/internal/tenants/{tenantRef}/plan`, `/billing/credit-purchases`, management's `/billing/business-overage`) are then reachable from the internet with only `X-Wbskt-Internal-Key` between a caller and a tenant's plan; `.env.example:130` ships a guessable placeholder for that key. `PublicRoutingTests.No_public_router_reaches_an_internal_route` only checks that the label text contains the exclusion, so it passes.

**Fix:** Replace the exclusion in both rules with a case-insensitive regexp, `!PathRegexp(`(?i)^/api/internal(/|$$)`)`, and make the test assert on a mixed-case path. Better still, add a host-side wall as well: a filter on the internal controllers that refuses a request that arrived through the proxy (a forwarded header from a known proxy).

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed there is no host-side guard: the controllers carry only `[Authorize]` or `[InternalApiKey]`, and nothing sets case-sensitive routing; that Traefik v3's `PathPrefix` is case-sensitive is from knowledge of Traefik, not from a run here)

### 60. A console pull-request run cancels the pending production deploy of a merged commit, and it happened twice on 11 Nov

P2 · Infra and CI · size XS · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#177

**Where:** .github/workflows/build-and-deploy.yml:17-19 (workflow-level `concurrency: group: console-deploy, cancel-in-progress: false`), :25 (`verify` on the one self-hosted runner), :45 and :89 (build and deploy only off pull requests); runs 139 to 145 of "Build and deploy console"

**Scenario:** The concurrency group sits on the whole workflow and is the same for pull-request runs and master runs. GitHub keeps one running and one pending run per group, and a newer pending run replaces an older pending one. On 11 Nov run 139 (a pull request) sat queued for the self-hosted runner from 18:43 UTC and was never picked up. Behind it, the master run 141 (90fa160, created 19:07:03) was cancelled at 19:38:24, one second after pull-request run 142 was created; the master run 143 (3605b5b, created 19:45:01) was cancelled at 19:59:43, one second after pull-request run 144. Neither merged commit was built or deployed, and neither run started a job. Run 145 (master 4633abb) was still pending when read. With a healthy runner the same thing happens whenever a pull-request push arrives while a master run waits. `cancel-in-progress: false` only protects the run that is already running.

**Fix:** Move the `concurrency` block from the workflow to the `deploy` job (as wbskt/Wbskt does in `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:44-46`), or key the workflow group by ref (`console-${{ github.ref }}`) and keep a fixed group on `deploy`. Then find out why the runner stopped taking jobs at 18:43 on 11 Nov and re-run master.

**Confidence:** high (the workflow text was read twice and the cancellations are in the run list with matching timestamps; why the runner is not taking jobs is unknown)

### 61. Every alert is raised from the VM it watches, so a VM, Docker or Alertmanager outage reaches nobody

P2 · Infra and CI · size S · #776

**Where:** deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:593-636 (Prometheus and Alertmanager run on the same VM as everything they watch); deploy/observability/alerts.yml (22 rules, none always-firing); deploy/scripts/alert.sh:15 (backup alerts go to Alertmanager on 127.0.0.1); deploy/infra-map.html:734-745 (`GAPS`, which names the single VM and disk but not this)

**Scenario:** The VM stops, loses its network, fills its disk to the point Docker stops, or Alertmanager cannot reach the mail relay. Every rule, including `BackupMissing` and the backup script's own alert, is evaluated and sent from that machine, so nothing is sent. There is no always-firing rule that an outside service expects to hear from, and no probe from outside the VM of the public endpoints. The outage is found by a user. The failed deploy of 10 Nov (port 22 timed out from GitHub) is the kind of event that nothing here would report.

**Fix:** Add a `Watchdog` rule (`vector(1)`, always firing) routed to an outside dead-man's-switch address that alerts when it stops arriving, and an outside HTTP check of `https://auth.<DOMAIN>/health/ready` and the console. Record both in the alert table and the infra map.

**Confidence:** medium (alerts.yml, compose, README and the map were searched for a watchdog, heartbeat or outside probe and none is described; one could exist outside the repositories)

### 62. No alert reads the failed-tick counter of the periodic jobs, and no alert fires when the auth, management or gateway hosts are unhealthy

P2 · Infra and CI · size S · #777

**Where:** Wbskt.Infrastructure/Hosting/PeriodicWorker.cs:51-66 and :90-92 (a failed tick is logged and counted, then the job carries on); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Telemetry/AuthMetrics.cs:52 and Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Telemetry/ManagementMetrics.cs:59-63 (`wbskt_*_worker_ticks_total{worker,outcome}`); deploy/observability/alerts.yml (none of the 22 rules names `worker_ticks`; the only liveness rules are `EngineNotReporting`, `ContainerRestarting` and `OtelCollectorDown`); deploy/observability/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/wbskt/overview.json (no panel for it)

**Scenario:** A periodic job that fails on every tick (the credit reconcile, the credit poster, the deleted-workspace sweep, retention) keeps running and keeps failing. The counter with `outcome="failed"` climbs and nothing looks at it, so credits stop being posted or deleted workspaces stop being purged until someone reads the logs. Separately, a host that is unhealthy but not restarting (auth answering 503 on its health check, a gateway whose broker connection is gone) is taken out of Traefik's routing and no rule fires: `ContainerRestarting` needs a restart, and only the engine has a "not reporting" rule.

**Fix:** Add two rules to `deploy/observability/alerts.yml`: `PeriodicJobFailing` on `increase(wbskt_auth_worker_ticks_total{outcome="failed"}[30m]) > 3` (and the management and workflow counters), and `HostNotReporting` on the absence of each host's own metrics for 5 minutes, modelled on `EngineNotReporting`. Add both to the alert table in `deploy/README.md` and to `ALERTS` in the infra map.

**Confidence:** high (alerts.yml and the dashboard were searched for every metric name the code registers; the counter names were read in code)

### 63. Mail leaving the auth host has no sent or failed metric, so a relay that refuses mail is invisible while sign-in depends on it

P2 · Infra and CI · size S · #778

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Email/OutboundMailQueue.cs:28-33 (bounded at 1000, in memory); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Email/OutboundMailDispatcher.cs:84-90 (dropped after the retries with a log line only), :68, :88, :92 (recipient address written to the log in clear); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Telemetry/AuthMetrics.cs:22 (the only mail instrument is `wbskt_auth_mail_suppressed_total`, the cooldown); deploy/observability/alerts.yml (no mail rule)

**Scenario:** The SMTP relay starts refusing mail (expired credential, sending limit reached, provider outage). Every confirmation, invitation and password-reset message is retried and then dropped. New users cannot confirm their address and so cannot sign in, and the only trace is an error line in Loki that no rule watches. The same lines carry the recipient's address in clear, which `deploy/README.md:247` says the logs do not hold (the malformed-address line at :81 does so on purpose and says why; the others do not).

**Fix:** Add `wbskt_auth_mail_total{outcome="sent|failed|dropped"}` and an observable gauge for the queue depth to `AuthMetrics`, count in `OutboundMailDispatcher`, and add a `MailFailing` rule (any `dropped` in 15 minutes) plus a dashboard panel. Log `EmailFingerprint.Of(address)` on the accepted, retry and dropped lines.

**Confidence:** high (the dispatcher, the queue and the metrics class were read twice; there is no counter on the path)

### 64. No container has a memory limit, so one leaking service gets SQL Server killed

P2 · Infra and CI · size S · #779

**Where:** deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml (no `mem_limit`, `deploy.resources` or `memswap_limit` on any service between lines 81 and 786); deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:547-571 (sql, bounded only from inside by `SQL_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB`); deploy/observability/alerts.yml:289-290 (annotation speaks of a container memory limit that is not set)

**Scenario:** Everything runs on one VM. A host with a leak, or Loki, Tempo or Prometheus growing under a burst of telemetry, takes memory until the kernel's OOM killer picks the largest process, which is SQL Server. Every host then fails at once, and the alert text that points the operator at "the memory limit" describes a limit nobody set.

**Fix:** Give each service a `mem_limit` in compose (hosts and gateways a few hundred MB each, the observability services their own), sized so the sum plus `SQL_MEMORY_LIMIT_MB` fits the VM, and expose the two or three that matter as `.env` keys.

**Confidence:** high (compose was searched for every limit key; none is present)

Registration answers `PLAN_CLIENT_LIMIT_REACHED` ("You have 20 clients; Free allows 20") for the other 180, the "Load tester" step fails, and the run has been red on 8d06049, ec1e0d4 and 5c7792c. It is the only run that puts load on the hosts, so it has measured nothing since the billing series merged, and nothing turns a failed scheduled run into an issue or an alert. Two smaller gaps in the same file: it still calls the unversioned aliases that #500 removes, and its health loop after the load checks auth, management, gateway-ws and the engine but not client-auth (5015) or gateway-mqtt (5021); neither nightly sends anything through the MQTT gateway.

**Fix:** Before registering clients, put the nightly account on a plan with room (the internal `PUT /api/internal/tenants/{tenantRef}/plan` with the run's internal key), move the two URLs to `/api/v1`, add 5015 and 5021 to the loop, and add a last step to both nightlies that opens or updates an issue when the job fails.
