### 66. Account mail lives only in memory, and the email-changed notice has no way to be asked for again

P3 · Auth and management · size S · #781

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Email/OutboundMailQueue.cs:9-24, 30-35; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Email/OutboundMailDispatcher.cs:16, 51-55, 71-76, 84-90; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Email/QueuedAuthMailer.cs:35-36; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/AccountProfileService.cs:198; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/RegistrationService.cs:250-279; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/PasswordService.cs:151-164

**Scenario:** Verification, password reset, invitation, email-change and email-changed mail waits in a bounded in-process channel. A mail is dropped when the relay fails three times in about ten seconds (2 s and 8 s delays), and everything queued is dropped when the host stops, which is every deploy. The queue's comment accepts this because "every message this host sends has a user-triggered way to ask for it again". Two things make that weaker than it reads. First, `QueueEmailChangedAsync` (the notice to the old address carrying the seven-day revert link, `AccountProfileService.cs:198`) has no such way: if it is dropped, the owner of the old address never learns the account's email changed and never gets the revert link, and that notice is the defence against a takeover through a stolen session. An invitation is in the same position from the invitee's side: only the inviter can send it again, and nothing tells the inviter it was lost. Second, the cooldown is claimed before the mail is queued (`RegistrationService.cs:250`, `PasswordService.cs:151`), so after a drop the user's next "resend" within 120 seconds answers as if sent and sends nothing. A relay restart of a minute therefore loses every mail asked for in that minute, while the billing notices beside it (`dbo.BillingNoticeMails`) wait in the database with a twelve-hour back-off.

**Fix:** Keep the in-process queue (the reason for it, raw tokens not going through RabbitMQ or a table, stands) but stop giving up after ten seconds: a mail that fails goes to an in-memory retry list with a back-off of minutes, up to its token's lifetime or an hour, so one bad mail does not hold the rest and a relay blip loses nothing. On shutdown, give the dispatcher a few seconds to drain what is queued before the host stops. For the email-changed notice, either record that it is owed (a column on the email-change row) and send it at next start, or release the cooldown when a mail is dropped so a resend works at once. Correct the comment in `OutboundMailQueue`.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the retry ladder, that `ReadAllAsync(stoppingToken)` ends the drain at stop with no flush, that the cooldown is taken before the queue write, and that no endpoint re-sends the email-changed notice; the queue is normally empty within a second, so the restart loss needs a mail in flight at deploy time)

### 67. State reports are applied in the order they are consumed, so an older report can overwrite a newer one

P3 · Auth and management · size S · #781

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/ClientMetadataIngestionHandler.cs:63-130 (the write at 103); Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/ClientStateVariables_Report.sql:20-28; compare ClientMetadataIngestionHandler.cs:202-203 (capabilities pass `message.CreatedAtUtc` and are refused when older); Events/Wbskt.EventBus.RabbitMQ/EventBusExtensions.cs:127 (in-memory retry, no partitioner); deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:340-351 (the client-auth instance runs the same consumers on the same queue)

**Scenario:** A client sends two state reports a few milliseconds apart (`{"relay":"on"}` then `{"relay":"off"}`), or the first one fails once (a SQL deadlock) and is retried by `ConsumerRetry.Default` after the second has been stored. The management consumers take messages concurrently, with no per-client partition, and production runs two processes on that queue (management and client-auth). `ClientStateVariables_Report` writes whatever arrives with `UpdatedAt = SYSUTCDATETIME()` and no comparison, so the older value is left as the client's current state, and the two `ClientPropertyUpdatedEvent`s go out in the wrong order with the wrong `OldValue`. The console and anything reading the state then show "on" for a relay that is off, until the client's next report of that variable. The capabilities path in the same handler was given an ordering guard (the 2 Nov review's follow-up); the state path was not.

**Fix:** Pass the report's time (`message.SentAtUtc ?? message.CreatedAtUtc`) to `ClientStateVariables_Report`, keep it in a `ReportedAt` column and update a variable only when the incoming time is not older. A refused variable returns `Stored = 1` with no change so no event is published for it.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed there is no ordering predicate in the procedure, no partitioner or concurrency limit on the endpoint, and that the client-auth instance registers the same consumers; how often two reports race in practice was not measured)

### 68. Every changed state variable is broadcast to the whole workspace group, outside the live-feed cap

P3 · Auth and management · size S · #781

**Where:** Events/Wbskt.Events/Client/ClientPropertyUpdatedEvent.cs:7-11 (`[SignalRNotify("OnClientPropertyUpdatedEvent")]` with the default workspace scope); Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/ClientMetadataIngestionHandler.cs:125-130 (one publish per changed variable); Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/SignalRForwardingHandler.cs:35-39 (workspace-scoped events skip `ClientFeedThrottle`); console `src/app/features/workspace/clients/client-tab/services/client-page.service.ts:171` (the only listener)

**Scenario:** A sensor reports ten changing values once a second. Each report becomes ten `ClientPropertyUpdatedEvent`s on the bus, ten rows in the traffic log and ten SignalR sends to every console connected to the workspace, whichever page it is on. Fifty such clients are 500 sends a second to each open console, and with the Redis backplane each is also a Redis publish. Only the open client page listens for this event, and it already joins that client's group (`JoinClient`) for the rest of the per-client traffic; `Docs/API.Endpoints.md` and the console's GEMINI.md both say per-client traffic stays off the workspace group. The 20-a-second cap that protects a console from a chatty client (`ClientFeedThrottle`) does not apply here because the event is not client-scoped.

**Fix:** Mark the event `Scope = NotifyScope.Client`. The console needs no change (it listens while watching the client). Because the cap would then be shared with the client's message rows, either count a state report's variables as one unit against the cap or send one `OnClientStateReported` event per report with the changed variables in it, which also cuts the bus and log volume by the number of variables.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the scope, the per-variable publish, the throttle bypass and that no other console code listens for the event; the send rates are arithmetic, not measured)

### 69. A bulk status change puts raw exception text in a 200 body and lists a committed change as failed

P3 · Auth and management · size XS · #781

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/ClientLifecycleService.cs:96-107, 116-119

**Scenario:** In `PATCH /clients/status` (the bulk status change), the status rows are written first and each change is then announced. If announcing one throws, the catch builds `Error.Failure("CLIENT_UPDATE_ERROR", ex.Message)` and copies `error.Message` into `BulkClientStatusFailure`, which goes out inside the 200 response. The conventions say a fault is not turned into `Error.Failure(..., ex.Message)` and that a `Failure` message is internal detail replaced before it reaches a client; that replacement happens in `MapError`, which this path never goes through. The row is also reported under `failed` although its status did change, so a caller that retries the failed ones repeats a change that is already in place. It is hard to reach today (the announce uses the queued bus and the cutoff store swallows Redis errors), which is why it is P3.

**Fix:** On an announce failure log it with the stack and still list the client under `updated` (the change is committed; the announce is best effort), or keep it under `failed` with a fixed message ("The status changed but could not be announced") and never `ex.Message`.

**Confidence:** high for the text reaching the body (second read of the catch and of the response mapping); low for how it can be triggered in practice

### 70. Event log retention is the one periodic job that also runs on the client-auth instance

P3 · Auth and management · size XS · #781

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Extensions/EventLoggingExtensions.cs:20; compare Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Program.cs:87-102, 124-128, 144-146, 160-162 (every other worker is registered only for `HostRole.All`); deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:340-351

**Scenario:** Production runs one management container and one client-auth container from the same image. `Program.cs` says the client-auth role keeps the registrations "minus the console's background work", and gates each worker on the role, but `AddDatabaseEventLogging` registers `EventLogRetentionService` unconditionally. Both containers start at the same deploy, so both run the same batched deletes over `dbo.EventLogs` and `dbo.ClientTraffic` at the same moments. The deletes are idempotent, so nothing is lost; the cost is doubled delete load, lock contention between the two sweeps, and a failed tick in the log when one is chosen as a deadlock victim. It is also the case #602 (singleton jobs behind a SQL lease) does not list, because that issue assumes the problem starts with a second management replica.

**Fix:** Register `EventLogRetentionService` in `Program.cs` under `if (role == HostRole.All)` like the others (pass the role into `AddDatabaseEventLogging`, or move the line). Add it to #602's list.

**Confidence:** high that it runs on both (second read of the registration and of the compose file); the contention is inferred

### 71. The 80% and 100% notices do not fire again after a plan change in the same month

P3 · Credits and usage · size S · #782

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Tables/WorkspaceMessageLimitNotices.sql:5-11`; `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Billing/MessageAllowanceMonitor.cs:61-75`; `Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingNotices.cs:26,34,90,104`

**Scenario:** A Free account passes 1.5 M messages on the 5th and is told at 80% and 100%. It upgrades to Maker (10 M). At 8 M and 10 M nothing is sent: the management host has already recorded 80 and 100 for that workspace and month, and the auth host has already claimed `Messages80` and `Messages100` for the tenant and month. The first thing the customer hears on Maker is the throttle notice at 20 M. Credits behave the same on the auth side: the poster does raise the crossing of the new allowance, and `CreditAllowance80`/`CreditAllowance100` are dropped as already sent for the month. The throttle itself does clear correctly: the next minute's snapshot is computed from the new plan, and it is empty on the 1st.

**Fix:** Put the allowance (or the plan revision) in the notice keys: a column on `dbo.WorkspaceMessageLimitNotices` and the period string the auth host claims.

**Confidence:** high for messages (both keys read); medium for credits (the auth claim was read in `BillingNotices.cs`, `dbo.BillingNotice_Record` itself is the billing lane's)

### 72. A sub-workflow child's start cost is charged to the root with no budget check, so a budget and a cap can be passed

P3 · Credits and usage · size S · #782

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:211-231,245-246`; `Docs/Billing.md:125,156-157`

**Scenario:** `Run_Start` adds the child's `@StartCost` to the root's `ChildCreditsConsumed` unconditionally (its comment says so) and seeds the child's own `CreditsConsumed` with it, which is what gets posted. A capped workspace's root run fans out with a parallel for-each over a sub-workflow step when it has 20 credits of ceiling left: 20 branches pass the step's own 1-credit charge, which takes the root to its ceiling, then 20 children start and add 20 more. The ledger ends 20 credits past the cap. Each child then fails its first charge as out of credits, so the overshoot is one start cost per child started at the edge. `Docs/Billing.md:156-157` says "a run cannot charge past its budget, so the workspace cannot pass its cap, however many runs it starts at once".

**Fix:** Take the child's start cost through the same check as any charge: have the sub-workflow step's charge include `RunStartCost` (one `RunCounters_TryCharge` for step plus start), and let `Run_Start` seed the child's own counter without adding to the root again.

**Confidence:** high (the unconditional update is at lines 220-224; the step costs 1.0 in `NodeKinds.cs:67` and the start costs 1.0 in `CreditCostOptions.cs:36`)

### 73. The credit month is decided by three clocks, and the ledger day is the day of posting

P3 · Credits and usage · size S · #782

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/RunCounters_TryCharge.sql:71`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_DeleteRetired.sql:53,89-91`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/RunCredits_Post.sql:80`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/WorkspaceUsage_GetSummary.sql:52`

**Scenario:** `Run_Start` takes the month from `@StartedAt` and the poster takes `@Day` from the injected `TimeProvider`, as the conventions ask. A lease extension takes it from `SYSUTCDATETIME()`. `Run_DeleteRetired` is handed `@NowUtc` from the engine's clock and still uses `SYSUTCDATETIME()` for the ledger day and the month it draws in. Grant expiry is checked against the SQL clock in the post and the usage summary and against the app clock in `Run_Start`. With the two clocks a second apart at 00:00 UTC on the 1st, a lease extension is measured against the new month's spend while starts are still measured against the old one, and a test that fixes `TimeProvider` cannot reach these branches. Separately, the ledger row is the day the credits were posted, not consumed: the last seconds of a month always land in the next one, and after a poster gap a whole backlog does (see the ended-runs finding). All of it is UTC; no local time was found.

**Fix:** Pass the app's now to `RunCounters_TryCharge` (or accept the SQL clock there and say so), and use `@NowUtc` in `Run_DeleteRetired` for `@Today` and the draw.

**Confidence:** high (each line read; the size of the effect depends on clock skew, which is small on one VM)

### 74. A node retried after a crash or a deploy is charged a second time

P3 · Credits and usage · size S · #782

**Where:** `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/RetryExecutor.cs:124-134,163-181`; `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BranchLoop.cs:154-158,235-245`

**Scenario:** The charge is taken before the node runs. If the engine stops while the node is running (a crash, or a deploy that interrupts the executor and leaves the branch Active for recovery), the recovered branch finds the idempotency key Pending, runs the node again and charges again. A replay-safe node has no key and is charged again the same way. The customer pays twice for one step because the platform restarted; with a deploy every in-flight webhook or email step (5 to 10 credits) is affected. Retries by policy are charged per attempt on purpose and are not part of this.

**Fix:** Skip the charge when the attempt's idempotency key already exists as Pending (the earlier attempt paid), and for replay-safe nodes when the branch is dispatched with `BranchExecutionReason.Recovered` on its first step.

**Confidence:** medium (the order of key check and charge was read twice; whether a second lease holder can also run the same node is the workflow lane's question and was not traced)

### 75. A run is admitted with less than its start cost left, and a free node still makes the charge round trip

P3 · Credits and usage · size XS · #782

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Run_Start.sql:162-166,191,245-246`; `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/RetryExecutor.cs:163-171`; `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/DefaultCreditCostCalculator.cs:32-34`

**Scenario:** Bookkeeping nodes cost 0.1, so a tenant usually ends with a fraction left. With 0.4 credits left the tenant check passes (`@CreditsLeft <= 0` is false; only the cap check compares with `@StartCost`), the run is created with a budget of 0.4 and `CreditsConsumed` of 1.0, and the trigger node's charge of 0 fails because spent is already past the budget. The customer sees a run that started and died as OutOfCredits and is 0.6 over. The same call shows a small cost on the hot path: every run's trigger node (cost 0) makes a `RunCounters_TryCharge` round trip that can only succeed or fail on money already spent.

**Fix:** Refuse with 50033 when `@CreditsLeft < @StartCost`, as the cap check does, and skip `TryChargeAsync` when the cost is 0.

**Confidence:** high (followed the fast and slow paths of `RunCounters_TryCharge` with `@Cost = 0` and spent above budget)

### 76. The hourly grant reconcile reads every credit pack ever bought and makes up to three writes for each

P3 · Credits and usage · size S · #782

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Billing/CreditGrantReconcile.cs:25-39,49-67`; `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Billing/CreditGrantReconcileWorker.cs:13-24`

**Scenario:** Each hour, on each management replica, the reconcile pages through all purchases from the auth host with no "changed since" filter and calls `TenantCreditGrant_Insert` for every one, plus `TenantCreditGrant_Revoke` for every refunded one and `TenantCreditGrant_SetHold` for every one ever disputed. The cost grows with the lifetime count of packs, including packs that expired a year ago, and each insert attempt takes `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK` on the unique index. Harmless at launch size; it is the only billing job whose work never shrinks.

**Fix:** Page by a change watermark (purchases changed since the last successful run, with a daily full pass), or skip purchases whose grant expired.

**Confidence:** high

### 77. The hourly review re-reads a tenant from Dodo for ever once it has an ended subscription on the plan it now pays for

P3 · Billing and pricing · size S · #783

**Where:** Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/TenantSubscriptions_GetToReview.sql:13-22, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/TenantBillingSync.cs:88-108

**Scenario:** A first checkout fails at the card (`subscription.failed` is stored), the customer tries again and the second subscription on the same plan goes active. Or a customer cancels Maker, lapses, and subscribes to Maker again. The tenant is now on a paid plan set by a subscription, and the old row matches `t.PlanCode = s.PlanCode` with a status of `failed`, `expired` or a past `cancelled`. The query does not ask whether that row is the deciding one, so the tenant is listed every hour; `ReviewAsync` reads the deciding (healthy) subscription from Dodo, upserts it and changes nothing. That is one provider call per such tenant per hour for as long as they stay subscribed, and an error log each hour while Dodo is down.

**Fix:** List a tenant only when it has no other non-duplicate subscription that is active, past due inside the grace or on hold inside the grace (a `NOT EXISTS` on the same table), or have the procedure return the deciding row's state.

**Confidence:** high (second read of the query and of `ReviewAsync`; the integration tests at Tests/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host.IntegrationTests/AuthDatabase/BillingPaymentProcedureTests.cs:289-327 cover one subscription per tenant only)

### 78. A Dodo timeout is a 500 and a Dodo refusal is a 503 to retry, and a timed-out upgrade does not put back the downgrade it dropped

P3 · Billing and pricing · size S · #783

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/TenantBillingService.cs:114, 169, 212, 254; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/DodoPaymentGateway.cs:216-223; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingWebhookService.cs:43

**Scenario:** Every provider call is caught as `HttpRequestException` only. (1) When Dodo takes longer than the client's 30 s, `HttpClient` throws `TaskCanceledException`, which none of the four catches takes: checkout, plan change and portal answer a 500 instead of 503 `BILLING_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE`, and in `UpgradeAsync` the scheduled downgrade dropped at line 207 is not rescheduled, which is the case commit 151acba set out to close. (2) `EnsureSucceeded` turns any non-2xx into `HttpRequestException`, so a 409 (a plan change already pending) or a 422 (a product id that is wrong or archived) is answered as 503 "could not be reached, try again shortly" with `Retry-After`. Coding.Conventions.md keeps `Unavailable` for a dependency that is down when nothing was done; a refusal on the merits retried for ever is not that. (3) The webhook's failure branch excludes `OperationCanceledException`, so a provider timeout during a webhook is neither counted as `failed` nor written to `LastError`.

**Fix:** Catch `TaskCanceledException` whose token is not the request's alongside `HttpRequestException`; map a 4xx from Dodo to a 409 `BILLING_PROVIDER_REFUSED` (keeping 5xx and network errors as 503); count a timeout as a failed webhook.

**Confidence:** high (second read of each catch and of `EnsureSucceeded`; no resilience handler is registered on the client at Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Program.cs:78-86)

### 79. A customer's declined card on a credit pack fires the "payment may have reached no tenant" alert

P3 · Billing and pricing · size XS · #783

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/TenantBillingSync.cs:233-236, Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/Billing/BillingWebhookService.cs:56, deploy/observability/alerts.yml:47-51

**Scenario:** A `payment.failed` (or `payment.processing`, `payment.cancelled`) event for a pack reaches `GrantCreditPackAsync`, which answers `SetAside("Payment ... has not succeeded")`. Every set-aside is counted as `set_aside`, and `BillingWebhooksSetAside` fires on any in an hour, with a description about money that reached no tenant. A declined card is the most common payment event there is, so the alert that is meant to catch lost money will mostly page for nothing and be ignored.

**Fix:** Answer `Done` for a payment that has not succeeded (nothing was taken), or give the metric a third outcome such as `ignored` that the alert leaves out.

**Confidence:** medium (second read confirmed the path from webhook to metric; which event types the Dodo endpoint is subscribed to is dashboard configuration and could not be read)

### 84. The built-in Admin role can be edited and deleted, and the last-administrator guard only watches users.manage

P3 · Tenants and access · size S · #784

**Where:** Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/Functions/Tenant_Administrators.sql:1-5 and 21-72 (administrator means tenant-wide `users.manage`; the comment says every management endpoint needs it, but the role endpoints need `roles.manage`); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/RolePermission_Remove.sql, RolePermission_Grant.sql, Role_Delete.sql (no check of `Roles.Kind`); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/Scripts/Script.PostDeployment.sql:263-276 (every deploy inserts each missing permission into every `Kind = 'Admin'` role); Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Create.sql:38-44

**Scenario:** (a) An administrator sends `POST /api/v1/tenants/{t}/roles/{Admin}/permissions {"permissionSlug":"roles.manage","isDeny":true}` (or deletes the slug). The guard passes because `users.manage` is still held. Now nobody in the tenant can change a role or an assignment, and nothing in the API can give it back: every route that could needs `roles.manage`. A deleted slug comes back at the next deploy through the backfill; a deny row does not, because the backfill skips a role that already has a row for the permission. The same loses `billing.manage` for a whole tenant. (b) The reverse: an owner who deliberately removed, say, `clients.firmware` from Admin gets it back silently at the next deploy. (c) `DELETE /api/v1/tenants/{t}/roles/{Admin}` is allowed while someone else holds `users.manage` directly; afterwards `Workspace_Create` finds no `Kind = 'Admin'` role and a workspace's creator gets no role in it.

**Fix:** Treat the two built-in roles as fixed: refuse `RolePermission_Grant`, `RolePermission_Remove` and `Role_Delete` for a role with `Kind = 'Admin'` (a THROW mapped to 409), and correct the comment in `Tenant_Administrators.sql`. That also ends the backfill working against an administrator's edit.

**Confidence:** high on the procedures and the backfill predicate (second read). Not run.

### 85. Unknown references inside a resolved tenant or workspace answer 403, and a foreign workspace answers differently from an unknown one

P3 · Tenants and access · size M · #784

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantReferences.cs:24; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/AccessControlService.cs:98, 264, 270, 302, 308, 399, 410, 430, 447-451; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantService.cs:194, 206; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/WorkspaceService.cs:141 (403 `USER_NOT_FOUND` on remove, while lines 107, 122 and 202 answer 404 for the same code); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/SqlGuardErrors.cs:62 with Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/UserRole_Assign.sql:16-19, UserPermission_Grant.sql:23-26, GroupRole_Assign.sql:21-24 (THROW 50002); Docs/Coding.Conventions.md "The ID Boundary"

**Scenario:** The convention is 403 for the scope (workspace or tenant the caller is not in) and 404 `<RESOURCE>_NOT_FOUND` for a reference inside it, with "403 inside a workspace only ever means a missing permission". The auth host's tenant administration answers 403 `ROLE_NOT_FOUND`, `GROUP_NOT_FOUND`, `USER_NOT_FOUND` for an unknown role, group or member inside a tenant the caller administers, and `DELETE /api/v1/workspaces/{ws}/members/{unknown}` answers 403 where `POST` on the same collection answers 404. A console that treats 403 as "you may not do this" shows the wrong message. Separately, `POST /api/v1/tenants/{t}/members/{u}/roles/{r} {"workspaceRef":X}` answers 403 `WORKSPACE_NOT_FOUND` when X names nothing and 400 `AUTH_OPERATION_REJECTED` "Workspace does not belong to the specified tenant." when X is another tenant's workspace, which tells the two apart (`CreateInvitation` maps the same case correctly through 50017, SqlGuardErrors.cs:44). Third, the auth host's own workspace routes tell an unknown workspace from one the caller is not in: `GET /api/v1/workspaces/{X}/permissions` answers 403 `WORKSPACE_NOT_FOUND` "Workspace not found." for the first and 403 `WORKSPACE_UNAUTHORIZED` for the second (Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/WorkspaceService.cs:278 and 285), although the convention says the two cases are not told apart; the management host does fold them into one `WORKSPACE_FORBIDDEN` (Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Clients/AuthServiceClient.cs:63). The E2E scenarios pin the 403s (Docs/Auth.Host.E2E.Scenarios.md `ROLE_10`, `GRP_05`, `MEM_09`, `WS_MEM_20`, `WS_RES_05`, `WS_RES_06`), so this is a decision to make, then one sweep.

**Fix:** Answer 404 for references inside a resolved tenant or workspace (keep 403 for the tenant or workspace itself), and resolve an assignment's `workspaceRef` with the tenant in the lookup so a foreign one is `WORKSPACE_NOT_FOUND` like an unknown one. Update the scenarios table with the code.

**Confidence:** high (second read of each line and of the mapping). Whether the convention is meant to bind tenant-scoped references as well as workspace-scoped ones is the judgement part.

### 86. Removing a workspace member accepts any user in the system and records a removal that did not happen

P3 · Tenants and access · size XS · #784

**Scenario:** A holder of `users.manage` in workspace W sends `DELETE /api/v1/workspaces/{W}/members/{userRef}` with the reference of a user who is not in W, or not in the tenant at all. The answer is 204 and a `WorkspaceMemberRemovedEvent` is published for W naming that user, so W's audit log and the console's live member list record a removal of someone who was never a member. A reference that names nobody answers 403, so the pair also says whether a user reference exists anywhere.

**Fix:** Resolve the user with `User_FindBy_RefIdInTenant` (as `AddUserToWorkspaceAsync` does), have `WorkspaceMember_Remove` return the row count, and answer 404 `USER_NOT_FOUND` without publishing when nothing was removed.

**Confidence:** high (second read of the service and the procedure).

P3 · Tenants and access · size XS · #784

The workspace now has an owner who cannot add a member, rename it or delete it, and who cannot be removed because the owner is protected (50006). A suspended member can be made owner the same way. A tenant-wide administrator can still repair it, so nothing is lost.

**Fix:** In `Workspace_SetOwner`, grant the new owner the tenant's `Kind = 'Admin'` role scoped to the workspace when they do not hold it, and refuse a suspended member (50009).

### 88. The reserved-name check drops every non-ASCII character, so a look-alike of a reserved name registers

**Scenario:** `POST /api/auth/register {"username":"аdmin", ...}` with a Cyrillic "а" (U+0430) normalises to `dmin`, which is not reserved, so the account is created and shows as "аdmin" in member lists and audit entries. `wbѕkt support` (Cyrillic "ѕ") normalises to `wbktsupport` and passes both the brand check and the reserved list. A name made only of non-ASCII characters normalises to nothing and is never reserved. The same check guards the rename in `AccountProfileService`. Reproduced the normalisation in Python; the C# was read, not run.

**Confidence:** high on what `Normalise` returns for these inputs; medium on impact (it needs a victim who trusts a display name).

### 89. A removed or suspended member keeps the workspace's live feed until the access token expires

P3 · Tenants and access · size M · #784

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Hubs/NotificationHub.cs:67 and 131 (the permission is checked once, when the connection joins `ws:{id}` or a client group; lines 92 and 150 are the only removals, both on the caller's own request); Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Program.cs:344 (`CloseOnAuthenticationExpiration = true`); Wbskt.Infrastructure/Security/AccessTokenRevocation.cs:15 (15 minutes); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantService.cs:87-109 and 147-182 (removing or suspending a member does not revoke their access token, which is right, since the account and its other tenants stay)

**Scenario:** An administrator sends `DELETE /api/v1/tenants/{t}/members/{userRef}` (or the suspend `PATCH`, or `DELETE /api/v1/workspaces/{ws}/members/{userRef}`) while that member has the console open. HTTP calls are refused at once, because the management host drops its cached access on the Redis announcement. The SignalR connection is not touched: nothing in the management host reacts to `MemberRemovedEvent`, `MemberSuspendedEvent`, `WorkspaceMemberRemovedEvent` or the access-change announcement except the event log, so the connection stays in the workspace group and goes on receiving every `[SignalRNotify]` event of the workspace (client messages, command payloads, run updates) until the token it connected with expires, up to 15 minutes later. Only the reconnect is refused.

**Fix:** On the access-change announcement (or on the three member events), have the hub re-resolve access for the connections in the affected workspace groups and remove, or abort, those that no longer pass. Tracking connection ids per user per workspace in the hub is enough.

**Confidence:** medium. The second read confirmed there is no code path that removes a connection from a group other than the caller's own leave; that events keep arriving on such a connection is how SignalR groups work, not something run here.

### 90. A failed event publish after a membership or role change returns 500 and skips the cache announcement

P3 · Tenants and access · size XS · #784

**Where:** Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantAnnouncer.cs:17-22 (`UserPermissionsChangedAsync` publishes with no catch, unlike `ToTenantAsync` at 29-39); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/TenantService.cs:103-108; Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Services/AccessControlService.cs:79, 328, 467 (`RolePermissionsChangedEvent`); Hosts/Wbskt.Auth.Host/Controllers/AnnouncesWorkspaceAccessChangeAttribute.cs:29-32 (announces only on a 2xx with no exception); Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Services/Clients/WorkspaceAccessCache.cs:33 (300 seconds for a confirmed entry)

**Scenario:** The broker is down or slow. An administrator removes a member: `TenantMember_Remove` commits, then the publish of `UserPermissionsChangedEvent` throws on the real bus (these services do not use the queued bus). The request answers 500, so the result filter publishes nothing to Redis, and each management replica goes on serving the removed member's cached access until the entry ages out, up to 300 seconds. The administrator sees an error for a removal that happened. The two events that cause this have no consumer anywhere (see "Dead or obsolete code"), so the publish that fails the request does nothing when it succeeds.

**Fix:** Stop publishing the two unconsumed events, or publish them best effort as `ToTenantAsync` does, so a committed change always reaches the 2xx and the Redis announcement.

**Confidence:** medium. The order of write, publish and announcement is confirmed by the second read; how the real bus behaves with the broker down (throw or wait) is inferred, not run.

**Scenario:** A user who received billing notices as a contact of a shared tenant deletes their account. The tenant is not deleted (others belong to it), so the cascade does not run, and the rows addressed to them stay: a row not yet sent is still sent to the deleted account's address on the next lease, and sent rows keep the address until the purge. The invitation rows show the intended handling (`User_Delete.sql:139-142` replaces the address).

**Fix:** In `User_Delete`, delete the unsent rows whose `ToAddress` is the account's address and blank the address on the sent ones.

**Confidence:** medium. The second read confirmed `User_Delete` never names the table; which members a notice is addressed to was not re-read.

### 93. A command the API accepts can be over the 64 KB message limit on the wire, and the SDKs drop the connection when it arrives

P3 · Gateways · size S · #785

**Where:** `Clients/Wbskt.Protocol/ClientMessageRules.cs:13-14, 37` (`MaxPayloadChars = MaxMessageBytes / 2`, counted in characters), `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Infrastructure/ClientConnectionBase.cs:54-57` (`JsonSerializer.Serialize(message)` with the default encoder), `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Handlers/ClientCommandHandler.cs:125-129, 134-139` (a client's own command payload goes out as a JSON string)

**Scenario:** The payload is sent as a string inside the envelope, and System.Text.Json's default encoder writes every `"` as `\u0022` and every non-ASCII character as `\uXXXX`, six bytes each. A payload of 11,000 Cyrillic, Hindi or CJK characters (a third of the allowed 32,768), or a 20,000-character JSON document with the usual share of quotes, passes `ClientMessageRules.Check` and leaves the gateway as a message of more than 65,536 bytes. The .NET SDK reads it as `FrameKind.TooBig` and drops the connection (`wbskt.dotnet/src/Wbskt/Internal/SocketClient.cs:119-121`); the Python SDK connects with `max_size=MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES` and closes with 1009 (`Wbskt.Python/src/wbskt/client.py:235`). The gateway has already announced the command as delivered and it is out of the queue, so it is lost, never acked, and the device flaps once. The protocol doc says the 64 KB limit holds both ways (`Docs/Client.Protocol.md:95, 109`).

**Fix:** Enforce the limit on what is actually sent: in `ClientCommandSender`, measure the serialized envelope (or bound the payload by its escaped UTF-8 size) and refuse with `PayloadSize`; and serialize outbound envelopes with `JavaScriptEncoder.UnsafeRelaxedJsonEscaping` so the wire size is close to the text size. Add the worst case (32,768 quotes) to `ProtocolRulesTests`.

**Confidence:** high on the arithmetic and the code path (second read of the rule, the serializer call and both SDK readers); not run.

### 94. System message types are matched case-sensitively in the gateway, against the protocol's own rule

P3 · Gateways · size XS · #785

**Where:** `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/Inbound/InboundMessageDispatcher.cs:22, 50, 76` (`handlers.ToDictionary(h => h.Type, StringComparer.Ordinal)`), against `Clients/Wbskt.Protocol/MessageTypes.cs:43-49` and `Docs/Client.Protocol.md:103, 120`

**Scenario:** A hand-written client answers a command with `{"type":"Sys.Ack",…}` or a ping with `SYS.PONG`. `MessageTypes.IsSystem` (ignore case) says it is a system message, the ordinal handler table has no entry, and line 50 drops it as an "unknown system message" at debug level. The command never shows as acked and the latency reading never arrives. The doc says `type` is "compared ignoring case", `MessageTypes.Comparer` exists for exactly this ("wherever the platform decides something by them"), and the management host's ingestion handler already uses it (`Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Handlers/ClientMetadataIngestionHandler.cs:53-57`).

**Fix:** Build the table with `MessageTypes.Comparer`, and add a `SYS.ACK` case to `InboundMessageDispatcherTests`.

**Confidence:** high (second read of the dispatcher and its tests: no test sends a system type in another case).

### 95. An MQTT client can switch off liveness detection (keep-alive 0 or hours long), and in production the gateway's own TCP probe only reaches Traefik

P3 · Gateways · size XS · #785

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Services/MqttConnectAuthenticator.cs` (the keep-alive in CONNECT is not looked at), `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Infrastructure/GuardedTcpServerAdapter.cs:100-107` (TCP keepalive 60 s / 10 s / 3 on the accepted socket, with a comment that it "notices a peer that vanished without a FIN ... even when its MQTT keep-alive is off"), `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:105-114, 438-447` (both MQTT entrypoints are Traefik TCP routers, so the accepted socket's peer is Traefik), `Docs/Client.Protocol.MQTT.md:29` ("Keep alive: Any ... The gateway also probes the TCP connection on its own")

**Scenario:** A board connects with keep-alive 0 (allowed by MQTT, and the doc says "Any") or with a very long one, then loses power or Wi-Fi without a FIN. MQTTnet's keep-alive monitor skips a connection whose keep-alive is 0 (`MqttServerKeepAliveMonitor`, `KeepAlivePeriod == 0`), and otherwise waits 1.5 times what the client chose (up to 27 hours). The gateway's TCP probes are answered by Traefik, which is alive, so they never fail. The client stays "connected" until Traefik's own handling of the dead client leg closes the backend connection, which this repo does not configure. Meanwhile commands are sent into the void and reported delivered, and presence triggers do not fire. The WebSocket gateway does not have this hole: its ping and 30 s timeout (`Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.WebSocket.Host/Program.cs`, KeepAlive 30 s / timeout 30 s) run end to end through the proxy.

**Fix:** Bound the keep-alive in `MqttConnectAuthenticator`: refuse 0 and anything over a few minutes (MQTT 5 can instead answer with a Server Keep Alive), and say so in the binding doc. Correct the comment and the doc sentence, which are true only without a proxy in front.

**Confidence:** medium on the gateway and MQTTnet side (read in MQTTnet `master`); low on how long Traefik takes to drop a dead client leg (not read, not run).

### 96. A will message on the client's own up topic is delivered as a message from the client, though the doc says a will goes nowhere

P3 · Gateways · size XS · #785

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Services/MqttGateway.cs:146-164` (every publish that carries the session's connection and names the up topic is enqueued), `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Infrastructure/MqttConnection.cs:84-125` (`ReadAllAsync` drains what was queued before `Ended`), `Docs/Client.Protocol.MQTT.md:30`

**Scenario:** A client sets a will on `wbskt/v1/{id}/up` (a natural thing to try: "tell the platform I died"). When its connection drops without a DISCONNECT, MQTTnet dispatches the will through the same publish interceptor with the session's items (`MqttConnectedClient.RunAsync`, after the receive loop ends and before the disconnected event is raised). The gateway sees the client's own connection and the right topic, enqueues it, and the session's receive loop hands it to the inbox before it sees the end of the connection. The will arrives as an ordinary client message: it is logged, can start a workflow run, and counts against the allowance. Whether it gets in ahead of `Ended` depends on timing inside MQTTnet, so the same client's will is sometimes delivered and sometimes dropped. A will on any other topic is dropped with a "published to a topic that is not its own" warning on every unclean drop.

**Fix:** Decide which it is. To match the doc, clear the will at CONNECT (or mark the connection as ended before MQTTnet dispatches it, by refusing publishes once the channel adapter has closed). To make it a feature, document it and deliver it reliably.

**Confidence:** medium-low. The gateway side is certain; the order of will dispatch and the disconnected event was read in MQTTnet `master`, not the pinned version, and not run. No test connects with a will.

### 97. A client that unsubscribes from its down topic keeps being "sent" commands that go nowhere

P3 · Gateways · size XS · #785

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Services/MqttGateway.cs:53-57` (publish and subscribe are intercepted, unsubscribe is not), `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Infrastructure/MqttConnection.cs:129-146` (the comment there: "An injected message with no subscriber is dropped without an error")

**Scenario:** The gateway subscribes the client to its down topic itself. A client library that manages its own subscriptions, or user code that cleans up on a mode change, sends UNSUBSCRIBE for that topic; MQTTnet honours it. From then on `SendTextAsync` still succeeds (the connection is open), so every command is announced as delivered and removed from the queue, and none arrives. Nothing shows why except missing acks.

**Fix:** Handle `InterceptingUnsubscriptionAsync` and refuse (or ignore) an unsubscribe from the down topic. One loopback test.

**Confidence:** medium (handlers read in full; MQTTnet's default of honouring UNSUBSCRIBE is from its `master`; not run).

### 98. An account over its monthly messages has its clients' `capabilities` and `state.report` dropped too, silently, and the protocol does not mention it

P3 · Gateways · size S · #785

**Where:** `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/Inbound/InboundMessageDispatcher.cs:56-67` (everything that is not `sys.*` takes from the one-a-minute allowance), `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Infrastructure/MessageThrottle.cs:58-88` (the minute is kept per client across sessions), `Docs/Client.Protocol.md` §4 Limits and §5 (only the per-connection rate is described, with close code Throttled), `Docs/Billing.md:230-235`

**Scenario:** A Free workspace is throttled. A device reboots after a firmware update and, as the protocol requires, sends `capabilities` first. If it sent anything in the previous minute the report is dropped, so the console keeps the old firmware version and command list until some later connect happens to win the slot; if the report gets through, the device's first reading is dropped in its place. `state.report` competes for the same slot. On the wire the device sees nothing: no close code, no message, and an MQTT QoS 1 publish still gets PUBACK success, so an SDK cannot tell its user why data is missing. `Docs/Billing.md` describes the throttle; the client protocol, which is what an SDK author reads, does not.

**Fix:** Let `capabilities` through like the `sys.*` types (it is the platform's own bookkeeping; it is one message per connection). Add a paragraph to `Docs/Client.Protocol.md` §4 saying that an account over its allowance has client messages dropped to one a minute without a close. Consider one `sys.notice` message to the client when the first message is held back, so SDKs can log it.

**Confidence:** high on the code path; whether `capabilities` should be exempt is a product call.

### 99. A command handed back by a gateway can wait a whole connection for nothing when the client is back on the same gateway, and the same drop seen as a send error fails the command instead

P3 · Gateways · size S · #785

**Where:** `Wbskt.Infrastructure/Clients/ClientCommandQueue.cs:78-91` (a target on the host that handed the command back is always treated as stale presence), `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Handlers/ClientCommandHandler.cs:45-58, 67-78, 96-112`

**Scenario:** (1) With one replica per gateway (the compose default) a client always comes back to the host that handed its command back. If the hand-back is consumed after the reconnect has been announced and its delivery has run on an empty queue (the requeue consumer retried on a SQL blip, or was behind), `QueueAsync` reads "connected on H", H is `returnedByHostId`, and the command waits for the next connect. A stable device may not reconnect before the command's 24-hour expiry, while the console shows it online with a queued command. (2) The same event, a client dropping between the sender's presence read and the send, has two outcomes depending on a few milliseconds: seen at line 46 the command is requeued and survives; seen at line 69 as a send exception it is failed as "could not be sent", even though it has an id and its sender was promised queueing.

**Fix:** Carry the hand-back time on `RequeueClientCommand` and deliver when presence's `ConnectedAt` is later than it (that connection is not the one that handed the command back). Requeue on a send exception when the connection reports closed, and fail only when it is still open.

**Confidence:** medium-low for (1): the code path is certain, the window needs the requeue to lag behind a reconnect. High for (2).

### 100. The MQTT listener holds any number of unauthenticated connections

P3 · Gateways · size S · #785

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Infrastructure/GuardedTcpServerAdapter.cs:51, 90` (every accepted socket gets a task; no cap, no per-address limit), `Hosts/Wbskt.Gateway.MQTT.Host/Program.cs` (no rate limiter), `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:112, 127, 443-446` (plain 1883 is published and routed for any source)

**Scenario:** Anyone can open TCP connections to 1883 or 8883 and send nothing. MQTTnet waits for CONNECT for its default communication timeout (100 s in `master`) before dropping each one, and each costs a socket, a 4 KB buffer and a task. Nothing counts or limits them, and a per-address limit is not possible today because Traefik's TCP router hides the client address (#655). The packet-size guard protects memory per connection but not the number of connections.

**Fix:** Set a short CONNECT timeout on the server options (a few seconds), cap concurrent unauthenticated connections in the adapter, and add the per-address limit once #655 passes the address through (PROXY protocol).

**Confidence:** medium-low: the adapter was read in full; the 100 s default was read in MQTTnet `master`, and I did not check whether the options builder is given a shorter one elsewhere (it is not in `MqttGateway.cs:49-51`).

### 101. The conformance suite passes SDKs that ignore several checklist items, and has no MQTT scenarios

P3 · Gateways · size M · #785

**Where:** `Tests/Conformance/test_conformance.py:148-165, 168-178`, `Tests/Conformance/fake_wbskt.py:70, 123-135, 198-217`, `Tests/Conformance/test_live.py:146-155`, `Tests/Conformance/README.md:14`, `Docs/Client.Protocol.md:244-262`, `Docs/Client.Protocol.WebSocket.md:82-88`, `Docs/Client.Protocol.MQTT.md:92-103`

**Scenario:** Checklist items with no scenario that can fail: (a) "drops the token after 4002": the test asserts the login count for every code except 4002, so both behaviours pass; (b) "waits 30 to 60 s after 4003": only an upper bound (70 s) is checked, so an SDK that reconnects at once passes; (c) "reuses the access token until a minute before expiry": the fake always answers `expiresIn: 3600` and no test runs near expiry; (d) "never sends a message over 64 KB": no scenario, and the fake has no size limit and never closes with 1009 on its own; (e) nothing sends `state.report`; (f) the whole MQTT binding checklist (five items) has no scenario and the fake speaks WebSocket only, while the README still calls MQTT "a future binding". On the real-host side, `test_live` checks that the first connection is dropped when superseded but not that the code is 4001, and no live scenario covers 1009, 4003 or 1001 (the last would have caught the shutdown finding above).

**Fix:** Assert a second login after 4002 and a lower bound after 4003 for harnesses that declare they can read close codes; add a short-expiry login and an oversize-send scenario; give the fake the 64 KB limit; record the close code in the harness's `disconnected` event and assert 4001 in `test_live`. MQTT scenarios belong with the first SDK that speaks MQTT.

**Confidence:** high (each gap checked against the test body).

### 102. Gateway.Core reads the wall clock directly in four places, against the one-clock convention

P3 · Gateways · size XS · #785

**Where:** `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Infrastructure/ClientConnectionBase.cs:20, 29`, `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Infrastructure/RevocationCache.cs:24, 34, 43, 53`, `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/Inbound/PongHandler.cs:34`, `Wbskt.Gateway.Core/Services/Inbound/ClientMessageHandler.cs:27`

**Scenario:** `Docs/Coding.Conventions.md` ("Time in contracts") says code whose behaviour depends on the time takes it from the injected `TimeProvider`. The rest of Gateway.Core does (`MessageThrottle`, `ClientCommandHandler`, `SupersedeMonitor`), but the revocation cache's retention, the ping round trip, `EstablishedAtUtc` (which decides which of two connections is superseded) and the `sentAt` plausibility window do not, so their tests cannot fix the time and `RevocationCacheTests` cannot test expiry without waiting. `RevocationCache` also drops an expired entry only when that client is looked up again, so entries for clients that never come back stay for the life of the process (small: one entry per revoked client).

**Fix:** Inject `TimeProvider` into the four classes; prune `RevocationCache` on a timer or on write.

**Confidence:** high (grep of `DateTime.UtcNow` over the three projects; these are all the hits).

### 103. Python reports connected only after the whole backlog has drained, and capabilities set in that window are not sent

P3 · Client libraries · size S · #786

**Where:** src/wbskt/client.py:266-286 (`_connected = True` and `on_connected` only after the buffer is empty); client.py:153-161 (`set_capabilities` sends only `if self._connected`)

**Scenario:** A Pi reconnects with a full buffer. Draining 1,000 messages at 8 a second after a burst of 50 takes about two minutes, and for all of it `client.connected` is false and `on_connected` has not fired, although the server shows the client online and commands are being delivered. An app that calls `set_capabilities` in that window has its new list stored but not sent until the next reconnect. An app that produces 8 or more messages a second never empties the buffer, so it never sees `connected` at all. .NET raises `OnConnected` as soon as the socket opens (SocketClient.cs:73) and the ESP32 library after the first burst (WbsktClient.cpp:309-318), so the three differ on what "connected" means and no document says so.

**Fix:** Set `_connected` and raise `on_connected` once `capabilities` has gone out, and keep a separate internal flag for "backlog drained" to decide whether a send may bypass the buffer. Send from `set_capabilities` whenever the socket is open.

**Confidence:** high (reading; the conformance run shows `connected` arriving after the 150 readings).

### 104. .NET credential file is written in place and readable by other users; Python's is atomic and 0600

P3 · Client libraries · size XS · #786

**Where:** src/Wbskt/FileClientStorage.cs:35-45 (compare Wbskt.Python/src/wbskt/storage.py:40-48)

**Scenario:** `File.WriteAllTextAsync` creates the file with the process umask (0644 on a default Raspberry Pi OS), so any local user can read the client secret. It also truncates and rewrites in place: a power cut during the one write that matters, straight after registration, leaves an empty or partial file, `LoadCredentialsAsync` reads that as "none", and the next start registers a second client. Python writes a temp file, sets 0600 and renames, and tests the mode (tests/test_storage.py:19).

**Fix:** Write to a temp file in the same folder, set `UnixFileMode.UserRead | UserWrite` where supported, then `File.Move(..., overwrite: true)`.

**Confidence:** high (reading; the default file mode is the runtime's documented behaviour).

### 105. .NET client ignores Retry-After on a throttled registration

P3 · Client libraries · size XS · #786

**Where:** src/Wbskt/Internal/AuthClient.cs:19-30 (any non-success is a `SecurityException`); compare AuthClient.cs:36-39 for login, Wbskt.Python/src/wbskt/client.py:352-353 and Wbskt.Arduino/src/WbsktClient.cpp:240-243

**Scenario:** A batch of new .NET clients behind one address registers at once and the per-address limiter answers 429 with `Retry-After` (the server sets it, Wbskt/Wbskt.Infrastructure/Problems.cs:132-135). Client.Protocol.md section 5 says "Login or registration answered 429: Retry-After + random 0-2 s". Python and the ESP32 library do that for both calls. The .NET client treats a registration 429 as an ordinary failure and retries after 0-2 s, 0-4 s, 0-8 s, straight into the same window. A related difference on login: with a 429 that carries no `Retry-After`, .NET backs off exponentially (WbsktClient.cs:231-234) while Python and the ESP32 library retry every 0-2 s without growing (reconnect.py:36-37, Reconnect.cpp:17-19). The suite's `test_waits_for_retry_after_when_throttled` only throttles login, with the header.

**Fix:** Throw `ClientLoginThrottledException(RetryAfter(response))` from `RegisterAsync` on 429. In Python and the ESP32 library, count a 429 without the header as a failure for the back-off.

**Confidence:** high (reading).

P3 · Client libraries · size S · #786

**Where:** src/Wbskt/Protocol/Capabilities.cs:25-40 (public `System` and `SystemCapabilities.Ota`); src/Wbskt/Internal/SocketClient.cs:170 (`sys.ota.update` passes through to the app); src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:249-254 (every `sys.*` send is refused)

**Scenario:** A .NET app lists `SystemCapabilities.Ota` in `UpdateCapabilitiesAsync`. The management API now accepts `POST {clientRef}/ota` for it, the SDK acks the command and raises it on `OnMessageReceived`, and the app has no call that can send `sys.ota.status`, because `SendAsync` refuses reserved types. The update stays without `accepted`, `rejected` or `installed` in the client's activity. Python drops `sys.ota.update` silently and never lists `ota` (client.py:302-303, 365-374), and the site says .NET and Python take no updates (sdks.html:54).

**Confidence:** high (reading).

### 107. ESP32 send() returns true for a message it has just dropped

P3 · Client libraries · size XS · #786

**Where:** src/WbsktClient.cpp:550-561 (`outbox_.push(frame, stamp); return true;`); src/wbskt/Outbox.cpp:32-37 (`push` returns false and counts a drop when the frame is bigger than the whole buffer); src/WbsktClient.h:120-122 and README.md:91, 265 (the documented contract)

**Scenario:** With the default 16 KB buffer, a sketch sends a 20 KB message (legal, under 64 KB) while offline, while a backlog is waiting, or after a burst has emptied the send bucket. `Outbox::push` refuses it and `enqueue` still returns `true`, so `send` reports "sent or queued" for a message that is gone. The same message sent while connected with an empty queue goes straight out, so the result depends on timing.

**Fix:** `return outbox_.push(frame, stamp);`, and say in the header comment and README that `false` also means "larger than the offline buffer".

**Confidence:** high (both functions read twice; the host tests cover `Outbox::push` returning false but not `enqueue`).

### 108. The three libraries name and default the same things differently, and 1.0 will freeze it

P3 · Client libraries · size XS · #786

**Where:** src/Wbskt/Internal/SdkInfo.cs:10 (`"csharp-sdk"`; Python sends `"wbskt-python"`, client.py:367; ESP32 `"wbskt-arduino"`, WbsktClient.cpp:398); src/Wbskt/Protocol/Capabilities.cs:13 (`IsRequired = true`; Python `is_required = False`, models.py:32; ESP32 `required = false`, WbsktClient.h:89); src/Wbskt/WbsktClient.cs:38 against src/Wbskt/IWbsktClient.cs:3-29 (`OnSuperseded` is on the class, not the interface)

**Scenario:** The agent name is stored per client (`ClientCapabilities.AgentName`) and shown in the console, so fleets will carry `csharp-sdk` next to `wbskt-python` and `wbskt-arduino` for as long as those devices live. A parameter declared without saying whether it is required shows as required when the client is .NET and optional when it is Python or ESP32. Code written against `IWbsktClient`, as a DI consumer would be, cannot subscribe to the shared-credentials warning, and adding the member after 1.0 breaks every implementer.

**Fix:** Rename the agent to `wbskt-dotnet`, default `IsRequired` to false, and add `OnSuperseded` to the interface, all in the next beta.

**Confidence:** high (reading).

### 109. Claimed runtimes and dependency floors that CI does not build

P3 · Client libraries · size XS · #786

**Where:** Wbskt.Arduino/library.properties:10 and library.json:28-31 (`WebSockets >= 2.6.0`, `ArduinoJson >= 7.0.0`) against .github/workflows/ci.yml:29, 78-81, 102; Wbskt.Python/pyproject.toml:10, 19-22 against .github/workflows/ci.yml:17-22

**Scenario:** The ESP32 library declares WebSockets 2.6.0 as its floor, but every CI job installs the newest 2.x, and the code now reaches into that library's protected members (`WbsktSocket::disconnect`, WbsktClient.h:35-43) and depends on the failure reasons added in 2.7.2 (WbsktClient.cpp:35-36). A user whose IDE already holds 2.6.x gets a build nobody has compiled. Python does this properly for websockets (3.10 with 13.0, 3.13 with the newest) but its matrix and classifiers stop at 3.13 while `requires-python >= 3.10` lets pip install it on 3.14 and 3.15, which are current. For .NET, `net8.0` and `net10.0` are both built and tested (tests/Wbskt.Tests/Wbskt.Tests.csproj:5); note only that .NET 8 left support on 10 Nov 2026, so the csproj comment calling it "the LTS most boards ship" (src/Wbskt/Wbskt.csproj:4) has aged.

**Fix:** Add a `compile` matrix entry pinned to WebSockets 2.6.0 and ArduinoJson 7.0.0, or raise the floors to what is tested (2.7.2). Add Python 3.14 to the matrix and classifiers.

### 112. A run whose last branch ended as the engine stopped stays Running for up to 30 minutes and then ends Cancelled

P3 · Workflow engine · size S · #787

**Where:** `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BranchEndings.cs:25-32` and `:37-44` (status write, journal entry and release are three calls on the same token), `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/BranchTerminator.cs:16-20`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/HostedServices/RunReaper.cs:33-36`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Runtime/RunFinalizer.cs:124-137`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/HostedServices/RunRecoveryService.cs:39`

**Scenario:** The last branch of a run completes: `EndIfLiveAsync` commits `Completed`. The host stops (or the process dies) before `ReleaseAsync` decrements `RunCounters.ActiveBranchCount`. After the restart no branch is `Active`, so recovery dispatches nothing, and nothing calls the finalizer. The run shows `Running` with every step done. Once it is older than `RunStuckThreshold` the reaper requests cancellation with `REAPER_TIMEOUT` and finalizes; a `Cancelling` run with no cancelled branch ends `Cancelled`. A run that did all its work is reported as cancelled, its parent sub-workflow step (if any) reads `childResult.status = Cancelled`, and events queued behind it wait until then.

**Fix:** In `RunRecoveryService.RecoverAsync`, finalize every non-terminal run that has no `Active` or `Waiting` branch and no bookmark (the `Run_GetStuck` predicate without the age cut-off) by calling `FinalizeAsync` directly, without requesting cancellation, so the status comes from the branches. Give the reaper the same rule and keep `REAPER_TIMEOUT` for runs that still have unexplained state.

**Confidence:** high for the sequence (re-read); the three calls are not in one transaction and nothing repairs the counter. Frequency is low: it needs a stop between two SQL calls.

### 113. One failed lease renewal stops the only engine

P3 · Workflow engine · size XS · #787

**Where:** `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/HostedServices/LeaderElectionService.cs:107-114`, `Wbskt.Workflow/Engine/LeadershipGate.cs:11-19`, `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:490` (`replicas: ${ENGINE_REPLICAS:-1}`)

**Scenario:** The leader renews every 10 seconds on a 30 second lease. One renewal throws (a SQL timeout, a deadlock victim, a dropped pooled connection). The catch block sees `IsLeader` and calls `StopApplication()` at once, although two more renewals would fit before the lease expires and `LeadershipGate` already stops the periodic workers 5 seconds before expiry. With the default single replica the whole engine restarts for one transient error: triggers and timers pause until the container is back, and every wake in flight is lost as described in the first finding.

**Fix:** On an exception keep the role and retry on the next tick; stop only when `clock.GetUtcNow()` has passed `LeaseValidUntilUtc` minus the safety margin, or when `TryAcquireAsync` answers false (the lease really went to another holder).

**Confidence:** medium. The code path was re-read; that a single transient SQL error is common enough to matter is inferred.

### 114. A send-to-client step built in the designer reports success when the command was never delivered

P3 · Workflow engine · size S · #787

**Where:** `Wbskt.Workflow/NodeExecutors/Actions/CommandNodeExecutor.cs:49-62` and `:147-159`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Workflow.Engine.Host/InboundAdapters/ClientCommandPublisher.cs:34-50`, `Wbskt.Workflow.Abstraction/Models/Nodes/Actions/SendClientMessageConfig.cs` (`DefaultAckTimeout` 30 s), console `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/workflow-tab/components/design/node-details/actions/send-to-client-config/send-to-client-config-form.service.ts:46-58` (`awaitAck` and `ackTimeout` are not on the form)

**Scenario:** The designer cannot set `awaitAck` or `ackTimeout`, so every step it creates takes the non-waiting path. That path sends the command with a life of `ackTimeout ?? 30 s` and continues by `default` whatever the publisher found: an unknown client, an inactive client or a full offline queue only publish a `ClientCommandFailedEvent` and return normally. A workflow that sends "close the valve" to a client that is asleep for a minute sees the step succeed and the run end `Succeeded`, while the command expired in the queue after 30 seconds. Features.md:63 documents the 30 second default only for the waiting mode.

**Fix:** In the non-waiting path, fail the step (`Fail("COMMAND_NOT_SENT", reason, retryable: false)`) when the publisher reports unknown client, inactive client or queue full, by returning the outcome from `PublishCommandAsync`. Give the non-waiting path its own, longer default life (or a separate `expiresIn` setting), and put `awaitAck` and the timeout on the designer form.

**Confidence:** high for the code path (re-read). Whether 30 seconds is the intended life for a command nobody waits on is a product call; the docs do not say.

P3 · Workflow engine · size S · #787

### 122. Auth assignment tables have no index on the columns their deletes and foreign keys search by, so two workspace deletes can deadlock

P3 · Database · size S · #788

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Delete.sql:20-23`, `Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/Tables/UserRoles.sql:9-13`, `Tables/GroupRoles.sql:10-14`, `Tables/UserPermissions.sql:10-14`, `Tables/TenantMembers.sql:10-12`, `StoredProcedures/Role_Delete.sql:23-32`, `StoredProcedures/Group_Delete.sql:23-25`, `StoredProcedures/Tenant_GetForUser.sql:9-10`

**Scenario:** `UserRoles`, `GroupRoles` and `UserPermissions` each have one unique index that starts with the user or group, and foreign keys on `RoleId`, `TenantId` and `WorkspaceId` with no index. Two people delete two different workspaces at the same moment. Each `Workspace_Delete` scans all of `UserRoles` to delete its own rows and keeps them locked. Its last statement, `DELETE FROM dbo.Workspaces`, makes SQL Server check `FK_UserRoles_Workspace` by scanning `UserRoles` again with locking reads (foreign key checks do not use row versions), and that scan stops on the rows the other delete holds. The other delete is stopped the same way on this one's rows. One of the two is chosen as the deadlock victim and its caller gets a 500. `User_Delete` runs `Workspace_Delete` inside its own transaction, so a deleted account can be the other side. The same scans happen in `Role_Delete` (by `RoleId`, three tables and `TenantInvitations`) and `Group_Delete` (`UserGroups` by `GroupId`). `Tenant_GetForUser` reads `TenantMembers` by `UserId`, whose key is `(TenantId, UserId)`, so it scans the table on every call. The full list of foreign keys with no supporting index is in the index table below.

**Fix:** Add `IX_UserRoles_WorkspaceId`, `IX_UserRoles_RoleId`, `IX_GroupRoles_WorkspaceId`, `IX_GroupRoles_RoleId`, `IX_UserPermissions_WorkspaceId`, `IX_TenantMembers_UserId`, `IX_TenantInvitations_RoleId` and `IX_UserGroups_GroupId`. The tables are small, so the indexes cost nothing to build.

**Confidence:** medium (the missing indexes are certain: scripted over every table, then read. The deadlock follows from how SQL Server checks a foreign key on delete, which was reasoned and not run; it needs two deletes inside the same few milliseconds.)

### 123. Three pairs of procedures take the same locks in opposite orders

P3 · Database · size S · #788

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database.Auth/StoredProcedures/TenantInvitation_Accept.sql:33-42,53` against `Role_Delete.sql:20,30` and `User_Delete.sql:38-40,139-142`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/TenantCreditGrants_Draw.sql:78-100` against `TenantCreditGrant_Revoke.sql:11-15` and `TenantCreditGrant_SetHold.sql:15-20`; `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Workspace_Retire.sql:29-38,43-45,75` against `WorkflowDefinition_Delete.sql:26-28,47-49` and `Client_UpdateStatuses.sql:42,54`

**Scenario:** (a) New in this range (fb7ca4b): an invitee accepts an invitation to a tenant whose plan has a member limit while an administrator of that tenant deletes a role. `TenantInvitation_Accept` locks the invitation row and then asks for the tenant row. `Role_Delete` holds the tenant row and then updates `TenantInvitations` by `RoleId`, which has no index, so it scans every invitation and stops on the locked one. One of the two fails with a deadlock: the invitee sees an error on the accept page, or the administrator on the role delete. `User_Delete` has the same shape (tenant rows first, then a scan of `TenantInvitations` by `Email`). (b) `TenantCreditGrants_Draw` reads a tenant's grants with `UPDLOCK` through `IX_TenantCreditGrants_TenantId` and then updates the rows by id; a refund (`TenantCreditGrant_Revoke`) or a chargeback hold (`TenantCreditGrant_SetHold`) locks the row first and then the same index key, because `RevokedAt` and `HeldAt` are included columns. If the refund is the victim its event is redelivered; if the draw is, the next 10 second post retries. (c) `Workspace_Retire` deletes trigger registrations and then the workspace's drafts, and updates policies and then clients; `WorkflowDefinition_Delete` locks the draft and then deletes the registrations, and `Client_UpdateStatuses` locks clients and then policies. These only collide for a request already in flight in a workspace that was just deleted, and the daily retire sweep repeats a retire that lost.

**Fix:** (a) In `TenantInvitation_Accept`, read the invitation's `TenantId` without a lock, lock the tenant row, then take the invitation row with `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK` and test it; add `IX_TenantInvitations_RoleId`. Correct the comment at `User_Delete.sql:34-35`, which still says accepting an invitation does not lock the tenant row. (b) Have `Revoke` and `SetHold` take the tenant's `TenantCreditMonths` row with `UPDLOCK, HOLDLOCK` first, as `Draw` does (`TenantCreditGrants_Draw.sql`, the month row is its first lock), or leave it and rely on the retries, and say so in a comment. (c) Leave as is; record the order in the procedure headers.

**Confidence:** medium (lock order read statement by statement in each procedure; which index each statement uses is inferred from the table definitions, not from a plan; nothing run; no deadlock graph to confirm any of them has happened)

### 124. The message allowance check scans the whole message ledger every minute

P3 · Database · size XS · #788

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/WorkspaceMessageUsage_GetAccounts.sql:11-15`, `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Tables/WorkspaceMessageUsage.sql:14`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Models/BillingOptions.cs:19`, `Hosts/Wbskt.Management.Host/Providers/MessageAllowanceProvider.cs:16`

**Scenario:** `dbo.WorkspaceMessageUsage` gets one row per workspace per day and nothing deletes from it (its header says it is kept after the traffic is swept). Its only index is the key `(WorkspaceId, Day)`. `WorkspaceMessageUsage_GetAccounts` asks for `WHERE Day >= @MonthStart GROUP BY WorkspaceId` with no workspace, so it reads every row of every month ever recorded, once a minute, on each management instance. With 2,000 workspaces that is about 730,000 rows a year, all read 1,440 times a day. Both the table and the procedure are new in this range (453c39f).

**Fix:** Add `CREATE INDEX IX_WorkspaceMessageUsage_Day ON dbo.WorkspaceMessageUsage (Day) INCLUDE (WorkspaceId, Inbound, Outbound)`, so the read touches only the current month. Decide how long the ledger is kept (invoices need the past months, not the past years) and prune it with the other retention jobs.

**Confidence:** high for the scan (one predicate, one index, read twice); the row counts are an estimate

### 125. `dbo.Clients` has no index for the presence sweeps or for the plan's registered-client count

P3 · Database · size XS · #788

**Where:** `Databases/Wbskt.Database/Tables/Clients.sql:27-31`, `Databases/Wbskt.Database/StoredProcedures/Client_ExpirePresence.sql:17-30`, `Client_ReconcilePresenceBy_HostId.sql:18-28`, `Client_Create.sql:37,43-51`, `Client_UpdateStatuses.sql:58,81-85`, `Client_GetCountBy_Account.sql:13`, `WorkspaceUsage_GetSummary.sql:34-38`

**Scenario:** `Client_ExpirePresence` runs every 60 seconds and `Client_ReconcilePresenceBy_HostId` (new in this range, 5501e97) runs for every snapshot a socket host sends. Both are `UPDATE`s filtered on `IsConnected` and `ConnectedHostId`, which no index carries, so each one scans all of `dbo.Clients` and takes an update lock on every row in turn. A registration or status change that holds a client row makes the sweep wait behind it, and the sweep makes them wait in turn. The count the plan limit uses (`COUNT(*) ... WHERE Status = 1` by workspace or by policy, taken while holding the tenant's `plan-limit:clients` lock) seeks `IX_Clients_WorkspaceId` or `IX_Clients_PolicyId` and then looks up every client of the workspace in the clustered index to read `Status`. At 100 clients none of this shows; at the 10,000s it is the cost of every registration.

**Fix:** Add a filtered index `IX_Clients_Connected (ConnectedHostId) INCLUDE (ConnectedAt, RefId, WorkspaceId) WHERE IsConnected = 1`, and add `Status` as a second key column of `IX_Clients_WorkspaceId` and `IX_Clients_PolicyId`.

**Confidence:** high for the missing indexes (read); the cost is unmeasured until #619

### 128. A policy created, enabled or deleted by someone else, and a client deleted by someone else, never reach the explorers

P3 · Console and site · size S · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#179

**Where:** `src/app/core/signalr/signalr-methods.enum.ts:39` (`OnPolicyCreated` declared, never subscribed; no member for `OnPolicyEnabledEvent` or `OnPolicyDeletedEvent`, which the backend raises in `Events/Wbskt.Events/Management/PolicyEnabledEvent.cs:9` and `PolicyDeletedEvent.cs:13`); `src/app/features/workspace/shared/services/workspace-data.service.ts:109-114` (the workspace-wide handlers are client presence, latency, rename, status and enrollment only); `features/workspace/policies/policy-tab/services/policy-page.service.ts:60-70` (`_policyMethods` has `OnPolicyDisabled` but not enabled); `policy-clients.service.ts:332` (the only `OnClientDeleted` listener, alive only while a policy tab is open)

**Scenario:** Two members have the same workspace open. One creates a policy, re-enables a disabled one, or deletes a client. The other member's policy explorer does not gain the new policy, an open policy tab keeps saying "Disabled", and the deleted client stays in the client tree (opening it then gives a 404) until a page reload or an unrelated enrollment event.

**Fix:** In `WorkspaceDataService`, subscribe to `OnPolicyCreated`, `OnPolicyEnabled`, `OnPolicyDisabled`, `OnPolicyDeleted` and `OnPolicyNameUpdated` (call `refreshPolicies()` or patch the row) and to `OnClientDeleted` (remove the row); add `OnPolicyEnabled` to `_policyMethods`.

**Confidence:** high (every `.on(` call site and the backend attribute list compared); P3 because Free and Maker workspaces have one member

**Also found by the auth and management lane:** The console never hears that a policy was enabled or deleted, or that a client was deleted by someone else. Two people have the same workspace open. One disables a policy and enables it again: the other's policy page handles `OnPolicyDisabledEvent` and shows it disabled, and stays that way because nothing listens for the enable. One deletes a policy or creates one: the other's explorer keeps the old tree, since the roster service listens only for client events (`OnClientRegistrationInitiated`, connect, disconnect, latency, rename, status). One deletes a client: only an open policy clients panel (`policy-clients.service.ts:332`) removes it; the explorer row stays and opening it is a 404. The same applies to one person with two tabs. A reload fixes each.

### 129. Renaming, approving, blocking or deleting a client refetches three lists the hub event has already patched

P3 · Console and site · size XS · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#179

**Where:** `src/app/features/workspace/clients/services/client.service.ts:109-111` (`reload()` calls `_data.refresh()`), `:180`, `:202`, `:215`; `src/app/features/workspace/shared/services/workspace-data.service.ts:83-88` (`_onRenamed` and `_onStatusChanged` patch the row), `:153-157` (`refresh()` reads policies, clients and pending)

**Scenario:** The user renames one client. The PATCH succeeds, the hub sends `OnClientRenamedEvent` and the row is patched in place, and `reload()` also issues `GET /registration-policies`, `GET /clients` and `GET /clients?status=Pending`, each up to 200 rows. A batch approval of 30 pending clients does this 30 times. GEMINI.md says "Prefer patching signals over refetching" and that renaming must not refetch. It is also what triggers the fall back to page one in the paging finding above.

**Fix:** In `rename` and `setStatus` patch the roster from the response (or rely on the hub event) and drop `reload()`; in `delete` remove the row locally.

**Confidence:** high (read twice)

### 130. Console models still name fields the API does not send, and miss ones it now does

P3 · Console and site · size S · wbskt/Wbskt.Console#179

**Where:** `src/app/core/api/workflows/workflow-api.model.ts:183-185` (`StartRunResponse.runId: number`; the backend sends `StartRunResponse(RunRefId, Outcome)`), `:111-117` (`CreateWorkflowResponse` declares `name`, `description`, `createdAt`; the backend's `WorkflowPublishResponse` is `(RefId, Version, Status)`), `:81`, `:89` (`workspaceId`, `publishedBy`, both `[JsonIgnore]` on the server), `:125-126` (comment lists "Completed" and "Faulted" and says the run summary has no trigger or credits); `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/workflow-tab/services/workflow-page.service.ts:108` (passes `res.createdAt`, always `undefined`, into the definition shown after a publish); `src/app/features/workspace/workflows/services/workflow.service.ts:179` (`workspaceId: parseInt(workspaceRef, 10)`, the integer parse of a GUID), `:187` (`publishedBy: 1`), `:243` (`running: w.active`, though stats now split `active` into `running` and `waiting`); `src/app/core/api/workspaces/workspace-api.model.ts:19-24` (`WorkspaceListItem` has no `tenantRefId`, which `WorkspaceResponse` now carries), `:26` ("It carries no owner flag"; `TenantMemberResponse.IsOwner` exists); `src/app/features/dashboard/members-page/services/members.service.ts:185-196` (add-member suggestions work only when the user is in exactly one account, because the model lacks `tenantRefId`)

**Scenario:** Three visible effects. A user who belongs to two accounts gets no suggestions when adding a member, although the workspace list says which account owns the workspace. After a publish the definition briefly holds an undefined `createdAt`. A workflow whose only active run is parked on a wait is counted as "running" in the explorer tally. The rest is noise that hides the next real contract change (the "Completed" finding is one such).

**Fix:** Bring the models in line with the `*.Models` records: `runRefId` and `outcome`; drop the three publish fields and use the request time locally; drop `workspaceId` and `publishedBy` from what is sent; add `tenantRefId` and `isOwner` and use `tenantRefId` in `resolveTenant()`; use `running` and `waiting`.

**Confidence:** high for the shapes (both sides read); medium for what the user sees after a publish (the server's copy replaces the local one a moment later)

**Fix:** Move each declaration to the `.model.ts` or `utils/*.util.ts` beside it, one folder per commit, and add an ESLint rule (`no-restricted-syntax` on top-level `TSInterfaceDeclaration`, `TSTypeAliasDeclaration`, `FunctionDeclaration` and `VariableDeclaration` in `*.ts` files that hold a decorated class) so it stops growing.

CODING_GUIDELINES.md says a service file contains only the class: no top-level `type`, `interface` or `const`; types go in the `.model.ts` beside it and a constant only the class uses is a `private readonly _name` member. These four files break it, and `MemberStatusFilter` at members.service.ts:16 is a string union for a value the code branches on, where the guidelines ask for an enum.
